confirms this trend. Notably, after adjusting for all confounding variables, the changing rate of CMR and its uniform 95% confidence band stabilize and remain consistently above 0 when PM
2.5
levels are below 8
$\mathrm{\SIUnitSymbolMicro g}$/m^{3}
, indicating a statistically significant positive association between PM
2.5
and CMR. However, for PM
2.5
levels above 10
$\mathrm{\SIUnitSymbolMicro g}$/m^{3}
, the association becomes less clear, potentially due to competing risks
(
Leiser et al., 2019
)
. Our findings align with those of
Wyatt et al. (2020a)
;
Takatsu and Westling (2022)
, while offering additional insights. Specifically, we estimate not only the relationship between PM
2.5
and CMR but also its derivative. Furthermore, our proposed estimators effectively address potential violations of the positivity condition (
A0
), yielding more robust conclusions in this observational study.

Figure 5
:
Estimated relationships between the PM
2.5
concentration and CMR or its changing rate at the county level.
Left:
The estimated CMR with respect to the PM
2.5
concentration.
Right:
The estimated changing rates of CMR with respect to the PM
2.5
concentration. We also present the 95% confidence intervals and uniform confidence bands as shaded regions and dashed lines respectively for each regression scenario.

6
Discussion

In summary, this paper studies nonparametric identification and inference on the dose-response curve and its derivative via innovative integral and localized strategies. Our proposed estimators consistently recover and infer these curves without assuming the overly restrictive positivity condition (
A0
) for continuous treatments. We establish the consistency and bootstrap validity of the proposed estimators when nuisance functions are estimated by kernel smoothing methods, without requiring explicit undersmoothing of bandwidth parameters. Simulations and real-world applications demonstrate the effectiveness of our approach in addressing the limitation of (
A0
). There are several future directions that can further advance the impacts of our proposal.

1. Estimation of Nuisance Parameters/Functions:
The proposed integral estimator (
10
) and localized derivative estimator (
14
) require estimating two nuisance functions: the partial derivative
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu(t,\bm{s})
and the conditional CDF
P(\bm{s}|t)
. When we focus on the kernel smoothing methods, the finite-sample performances of these estimators depend on the selection of three bandwidth parameters
h,b,\hslash>0
. While this paper employs rule-of-thumb and normal reference bandwidth selection methods, exploring other bandwidth choices through the plug-in rule
(
Ruppert et al., 1995
)
or cross-validation
(
Li and Racine, 2004
)
remains an interesting area for future research. More broadly, alternative approaches for estimating the nuisance functions, such as regression splines for
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu(t,\bm{s})

(
Friedman, 1991
;
Zhou and Wolfe, 2000
)
or nearest neighbor-type and local logistic methods for
P(\bm{s}|t)

(
Stute, 1986
;
Hall et al., 1999
)
, could further enhance the empirical performances of our proposed estimators
\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)
and
\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)
.

2. IPW and Doubly Robust Estimation:
Our proposed estimators (
10
) and (
14
) are based on the idea of regression adjustment. An interesting avenue for future research would be exploring how our integral and localized techniques could address the positivity requirement in existing generalized propensity score-based approaches
(
Hirano and Imbens, 2004
;
Imai and van Dyk, 2004
)
and doubly robust methods
(
Kennedy et al., 2017
;
Westling et al., 2020
;
Colangelo and Lee, 2020
;
Semenova and Chernozhukov, 2021
;
Bonvini and Kennedy, 2022
)
for estimating the dose-response curve and its derivative.

3. Additive Model Diagnostics:
Assumption
A2
is vital to the consistency of our proposed estimators, though its validity beyond the additive confounding model (
7
) remains unclear. Nevertheless, our estimators can be utilized to test the additive structure in (
7
). Under the positivity condition (
A0
), this can be achieved by estimating the absolute difference
\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu(t,\bm{S})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu(t,\bm{S})\big|T=t\right]\right|
from 0 using the RA estimator
\widehat{\theta}_{RA}(t)
in Remark
1
and our estimator
\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)
in (
14
). Other testing procedures, such as the marginal integration regression method
(
Linton and Nielsen, 1995
)
, are also applicable. When the positivity condition (
A0
) is violated, the partial derivative
\beta_{2}(t,\bm{s})=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu(t,\bm{s})
remains independent of
\bm{s}
for any
t\in\mathcal{T}
under (
7
). Consequently, our estimator
\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})
in (
12
) should asymptotically not depend on
\bm{s}
. This suggests that statistical quantities, such as
\Theta(t)=\sup_{\bm{s}\in\mathcal{S}(t)}\left|\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})-\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)\right|
and
\Theta=\int_{\mathcal{T}}\Theta(t)\,dt
, should converge to 0 as
n\to\infty
. Thus, by deriving the limiting distribution of
\Theta(t)
or
\Theta
, one can develop procedures to statistically test the additive confounding model (
7
) without relying on (
A0
). In contrast, existing tests for additivity
(
Eubank et al., 1995
;
Gozalo and Linton, 2001
)
generally require the positivity condition (
A0
) and may not be directly applicable here.

4. Violation of Ignorability (Assumption
A1
(b)):
In observational studies, unmeasured confounding variables beyond the covariate vector
\bm{S}\in\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
can bias inference
(
VanderWeele, 2008
)
. Hence, it is crucial to analyze the sensitivity of the dose-response curve and its derivative to violations of ignorability. In this context, approaches such as the instrumental variable model
(
Kilbertus et al., 2020
)
and the Riesz-Frechet representation technique
(
Chernozhukov et al., 2022
)
could be useful for the analysis. Alternatively, one could incorporate an additional high-dimensional covariate vector
\bm{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{\prime}}
into model (
7
) to mitigate unmeasured confounding variables
(
Guo et al., 2019
)
. We will leave a rigorous investigation of these directions as future work.

Supplementary Materials to “Nonparametric Inference on Dose-Response Curves Without the Positivity Condition”

Contents

Appendix A
Nonparametric Bounds Under Zero Treatment Variations

In this section, we study nonparametric bounds on the dose-response curve
m(t)
and its derivative function
\theta(t)
under the additive confounding model (
7
) when the intrinsic treatment variation has zero variance. Specifically,

Y(t)=\bar{m}(t)+\eta(\bm{S})+\epsilon\quad\text{ and }\quad T=f(\bm{S},E),

(21)

where
E\in\mathbb{R}
is an independent treatment variation random variable with
\mathbb{E}(E)=0
. When the treatment variation has zero variance, it means that
\mathrm{Var}(E)=0
.
The derivations of nonparametric bounds for more general models are left as future work.

To motivate this discussion, we first present an illustrative example demonstrating how zero treatment variation for certain covariate levels
\bm{s}\in\mathcal{S}
can lead to ambiguities in defining the corresponding dose-response curves.

Example 1

(Necessity of Assumption
A1
(c))
.

Suppose that the positivity condition (
A0
) holds, and let
T=f(\bm{S},E)=S_{1}
almost surely so that
\mathrm{Var}(E)=\mathrm{Var}(T|\bm{S}=\bm{s})=0
for any
S_{1}=s_{1}
. Here,
S_{1}
is the first component of
\bm{S}\in\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
E\in\mathbb{R}
is an independent treatment variation random variable. We further assume that
\mathbb{E}(S_{1})=0
and consider two equivalent conditional mean outcome functions:

\displaystyle\mu_{1}(T,\bm{S})\equiv T+2S_{1}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mu_{2}(T,\bm{S})\equiv 2T+S_{1},

both of which are well-defined under the positivity condition (
A0
) and equal to
3S_{1}
almost surely. While they agree on the support
\left\{(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{S}:t=f(\bm{s})=s_{1}\right\}
, these two conditional mean outcome functions lead to two distinct covariate-adjusted functions (or equivalently, dose-response curves):

m_{1}(t)=\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[Y_{1}(t)\right]=\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[\mu_{1}(t,\bm{S})\right]=t\quad\text{ and }\quad m_{2}(t)=\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[Y_{2}(t)\right]=\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[\mu_{2}(t,\bm{S})\right]=2t,

whose derivatives are different as well.

A.1
Nonparametric Bound on
m(t)

Under the additive confounding model (
21
), Example
1
above demonstrates that when
\mathrm{Var}(E)=0
, the conditional mean outcome function
\mu(t,\bm{s})
is identifiable only on the lower-dimensional surface
\left\{(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{S}:t=f(\bm{s},0)\equiv f(\bm{s})\right\}
, where

\mu\left(f(\bm{s}),\bm{s}\right)=\bar{m}\left(f(\bm{s})\right)+\eta(\bm{s})=m\left(f(\bm{s})\right)+\eta(\bm{s})

(22)

when
\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[\eta(\bm{S})\right]=0
by Proposition
2
.

Since
\mathrm{Var}(E)=0
, the relation
T=f(\bm{S})
can always be estimated from the observed data. Hence, we assume that the function
f
is known for any
\bm{s}\in\mathcal{S}
in subsequent analyses. To obtain nonparametric bounds for all possible values of
m(t)
, we impose the following assumption on the magnitude of the random effect function
\eta:\mathcal{S}\to\mathbb{R}
in (
21
); see
Manski (1990)
for related discussions.

Assumption A7

(Bounded random effect)
.

Let
L_{f}(t)=\left\{\bm{s}\in\mathcal{S}:f(\bm{s})=t\right\}
be a level set of the function
f:\mathcal{S}\to\mathbb{R}
at
t\in\mathcal{T}
. There exists a constant
\rho_{1}>0
such that

\rho_{1}\geq\max\left\{\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\bm{s}\in L_{f}(t)}|\eta(\bm{s})|,\;\frac{\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\bm{s}\in L_{f}(t)}\mu(f(\bm{s}),\bm{s})-\inf_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\inf_{\bm{s}\in L_{f}(t)}\mu(f(\bm{s}),\bm{s})}{2}\right\}.

By (
22
) and the first lower bound on
\rho_{1}\geq\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\bm{s}\in L_{f}(t)}|\eta(\bm{s})|
in Assumption
A7
, we know that

\left|\mu(f(\bm{s}),\bm{s})-m(t)\right|=|\eta(\bm{s})|\leq\rho_{1}

for any
\bm{s}\in L_{f}(t)
. This also implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}m(t)&\in\bigcap_{\bm{s}\in L_{f}(t)}\left[\mu(f(\bm{s}),\bm{s})-\rho_{1},\,\mu(f(\bm{s}),\bm{s})+\rho_{1}\right]\\
&=\left[\sup_{\bm{s}\in L_{f}(t)}\mu(f(\bm{s}),\bm{s})-\rho_{1},\,\inf_{\bm{s}\in L_{f}(t)}\mu(f(\bm{s}),\bm{s})+\rho_{1}\right],\end{split}

(23)

which is the nonparametric bound on
m(t)
that contains all the possible values of
m(t)
for any fixed
t\in\mathcal{T}
when
\mathrm{Var}(E)=0
. Notice that this bound (
23
) is well-defined and nonempty under the second lower bound on
\rho_{1}
in Assumption
A7
.

A.2
Nonparametric Bound on
\theta(t)

Since the functions
\mu(f(\bm{s}),\bm{s})
and
f(\bm{s})
are identifiable even when
\mathrm{Var}(E)=0
, their derivatives with respect to
\bm{s}
are also identifiable. Hence, we assume that the gradients
\nabla_{\bm{s}}\mu(f(\bm{s}),\bm{s})
and
\nabla f(\bm{s})
are known for any
\bm{s}\in\mathcal{S}
in the following analysis. The nonparametric bound on the dose-response curve
m(t)
relies on an upper bound on the random effect function
\eta:\mathcal{S}\to\mathbb{R}
(recall Assumption
A7
), while the nonparametric bound on the derivative function
\theta(t)
requires some constraints on the gradient
\nabla\eta(\bm{s})
of the random effect function as follows.

Assumption A8

(Constraints on the random effect gradient)
.

Let
L_{f}(t)=\left\{\bm{s}\in\mathcal{S}:f(\bm{s})=t\right\}
be a level set of the function
f:\mathcal{S}\to\mathbb{R}
at
t\in\mathcal{T}
. There exist constants
\rho_{2},\rho_{3}>0
such that

\rho_{3}\leq\inf_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\inf_{\bm{s}\in L_{f}(t)}\left|\left|\nabla\eta(\bm{s})\right|\right|_{\min}\leq\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\bm{s}\in L_{f}(t)}\left|\left|\nabla\eta(\bm{s})\right|\right|_{\max}\leq\rho_{2}

and

\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\bm{s}\in L_{f}(t)}\max_{j=1,...,d}\left[\frac{v_{j}(\bm{s})-\mathrm{sign}(g_{j}(\bm{s}))\cdot\rho_{2}}{g_{j}(\bm{s})}\right]\leq\inf_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\inf_{\bm{s}\in L_{f}(t)}\min_{j=1,...,d}\left[\frac{v_{j}(\bm{s})+\mathrm{sign}(g_{j}(\bm{s}))\cdot\rho_{2}}{g_{j}(\bm{s})}\right],

where
\left|\left|\nabla\eta(\bm{s})\right|\right|_{\min}=\min_{j=1,...,d}\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j}}\eta(\bm{s})\right|
,
\left|\left|\nabla\eta(\bm{s})\right|\right|_{\max}=\max_{j=1,...,d}\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j}}\eta(\bm{s})\right|
,
v_{j}(\bm{s})=\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j}}\mu(f(\bm{s}),\bm{s})
, and
g_{j}(\bm{s})=\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j}}f(\bm{s})
for
j=1,...,d
.

The first inequality in Assumption
A8
ensures that we can derive a finite and meaningful nonparametric bound on
\theta(t)
for any
t\in\mathcal{T}
. In particular, a direct calculation shows that

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{s}}\mu(f(\bm{s}),\bm{s})

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\bm{s}}m(f(\bm{s}))+\nabla\eta(\bm{s})

\displaystyle=m^{\prime}(f(\bm{s}))\cdot\nabla f(\bm{s})+\nabla\eta(\bm{s})

\displaystyle=\theta(t)\cdot\nabla f(\bm{s})+\nabla\eta(\bm{s})

for all
\bm{s}\in L_{f}(t)
. This indicates that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j}}\mu(f(\bm{s}),\bm{s})-\theta(t)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j}}f(\bm{s})\right|=\left|v_{j}(\bm{s})-\theta(t)\cdot g_{j}(\bm{s})\right|=\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j}}\eta(\bm{s})\right|\leq\rho_{2}

for
j=1,...,d
and therefore, a nonparametric bound on
\theta(t)
is given by

\theta(t)\in\bigcap_{j=1}^{d}\left[\frac{v_{j}(\bm{s})-\mathrm{sign}(g_{j}(\bm{s}))\cdot\rho_{2}}{g_{j}(\bm{s})},\;\frac{v_{j}(\bm{s})+\mathrm{sign}(g_{j}(\bm{s}))\cdot\rho_{2}}{g_{j}(\bm{s})}\right].

(24)

This bound is well-defined and nonempty under the second inequality in Assumption
A8
. More importantly, the nonparametric bound (
24
) highlights the pivotal role of the covariate effect function
f
of
\bm{S}
on treatment
T
in bounding the possible value of
\theta(t)
. Specifically, as the variation in
f
increases (
i.e.
, the magnitude of
\nabla f(\bm{s})
becomes larger), the nonparametric bound (
24
) becomes tighter, providing more precise constraints on
\theta(t)
.

Appendix B
Pointwise Consistency of
\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\mathbf{s})
from (
12
)

In this section, we establish the pointwise consistency of
\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})
from local polynomial regression (
12
).

B.1
Additional Notations

We introduce some notations that will be used in the following proofs of the consistency results of
\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})
from local polynomial regression (
12
) (Lemmas
B.1
and
3
). Under the notations in Assumption
A6
(a), we define a matrix

\bm{M}_{q}=\begin{pmatrix}\left(\kappa_{i+j-2}^{(T)}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq q+1}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}&\left(\kappa_{2,i-q-1}^{(S)}\mathbbm{1}_{\{i=j\}}\right)_{q+1<i,j\leq q+1+d}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1+d)\times(q+1+d)}.

(25)

Notice that
\bm{M}_{q}
only depends on the kernel functions
K_{T},K_{S}
in the local polynomial regression. For any
(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{S}
, we also define the functions
\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{q+1+d}
and
\bm{\psi}_{t,\bm{s}}:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{q+1+d}
as:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}(y,z,\bm{v})&=\begin{bmatrix}\left(y\cdot\left(\frac{z-t}{h}\right)^{j-1}K_{T}\left(\frac{z-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{v}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)\right)_{1\leq j\leq q+1}\\
\left(y\cdot\left(\frac{v_{j-q-1}-s_{j-q-1}}{b}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{z-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{v}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)\right)_{q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\end{bmatrix}\\
&=y\cdot\begin{bmatrix}\left(\left(\frac{z-t}{h}\right)^{j-1}K_{T}\left(\frac{z-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{v}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)\right)_{1\leq j\leq q+1}\\
\left(\left(\frac{v_{j-q-1}-s_{j-q-1}}{b}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{z-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{v}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)\right)_{q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\end{bmatrix}\equiv y\cdot\bm{\psi}_{t,\bm{s}}(z,\bm{v}).\end{split}

(26)

B.2
Consistency Statement and its Proof

The detailed asymptotic expressions for conditional variance and bias of
\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})
are stated in Lemma
B.1
below. Asymptotically, under the assumptions in Lemma
B.1
, we know that as
h,b,\frac{\max\{h,b\}^{4}}{h}\to 0
, and
nh^{3}b^{d}\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})-\beta_{2}(t,\bm{s})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}O\left(h^{q+1}+b^{2}+\frac{b^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nh^{3}b^{d}}}\right)&\text{ if }q\text{ is odd and }(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}^{\circ},\\
O\left(h^{q}+b^{2}+\frac{b^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nh^{3}b^{d}}}\right)&\text{ if }q\text{ is even and }(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}^{\circ},\\
O\left(h^{q}+\frac{\max\{h,b\}^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nh^{3}b^{d}}}\right)&\text{ if }q\text{ is an integer and }(t,\bm{s})\in\partial\mathcal{E}\end{cases}

for any fixed
(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}
and integer
q>0
.

Lemma B.1

(Pointwise convergence of
\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})
)
.

Suppose that Assumptions
A3
,
A4
,
A5
, and
A6
(a) hold. Let
\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})
be the second element of
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}(t,\bm{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{q+1}
,
\beta_{2}(t,\bm{s})=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu(t,\bm{s})
, and
\mathbb{X}=\left\{(T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})\right\}_{i=1}^{n}
. Then, for any
(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}^{\circ}
and
q>0
, as
h,b,\frac{b^{4}}{h}\to 0
and
nh^{3}b^{d}\to\infty
, we know that the asymptotic conditional covariance of
\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\in\mathbb{R}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\big|\mathbb{X}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{nh^{3}b^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\left[\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{M}_{q}^{*}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{e}_{2}+O\left(\max\{h,b\}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right],

and the asymptotic conditional bias is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Bias}\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\big|\mathbb{X}\right]=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{p(t,\bm{s})}\left[h^{q+1}\tau_{q}^{\text{odd}}+b^{2}\tau_{q}^{*}+O\left(\frac{b^{4}}{h}\right)+o_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]&q\text{ is odd},\\
\frac{1}{p(t,\bm{s})}\left[h^{q}\tau_{q}^{\text{even}}+b^{2}\tau_{q}^{*}+O\left(\frac{b^{4}}{h}\right)+o_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]&q\text{ is even}.\end{cases}

Here,
\mathcal{E}^{\circ}
is the interior of the support
\mathcal{E}\subset\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{S}
,
\bm{e}_{2}=(0,1,0,...,0)^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{q+1}
,
\bm{M}_{q}=\left(\kappa_{i+j-2}^{(T)}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq q+1}\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1)\times(q+1)}
,
\bm{M}_{q}^{*}=\left(\nu_{i+j-2}^{(T)}\nu_{0}^{(S)}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq q+1}\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1)\times(q+1)}
,
\tau_{q}^{\text{odd}}=\frac{\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\tau}_{q}}{h}
when
q
is odd and
\tau_{q}^{\text{even}}=\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\tau}_{q}
when
q
is even, as well as
\tau_{q}^{*}=\frac{\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\tau}_{q}^{*}}{b}
, where

\displaystyle\bm{\tau}_{q}=\left[\frac{\partial^{q+1}}{\partial t^{q+1}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\frac{1}{(q+1)!}\begin{bmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{q+j}^{(T)}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})+h\cdot\kappa_{q+j+1}^{(T)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})\end{pmatrix}_{1\leq j\leq q+1}\\
\begin{pmatrix}b\kappa_{q+1}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,j-q-1}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j-q-1}}p(t,\bm{s})\end{pmatrix}_{q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1+d)\times(q+1+d)},

\displaystyle\bm{\tau}_{q}^{*}=\begin{bmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}b\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t\partial s_{\ell}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\kappa_{j}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,\ell}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{\ell}}p(t,\bm{s})\end{pmatrix}_{1\leq j\leq q+1}\\
\begin{pmatrix}h\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t\partial s_{j-q-1}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\kappa_{2}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,j-q-1}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})\end{pmatrix}_{q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1+d)\times(q+1+d)},

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{\tau}}_{q}=\begin{bmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{j-1}^{(T)}p(t,\bm{s})\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\frac{\kappa_{2,\ell}^{(S)}}{2}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial s_{\ell}^{2}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right)\end{pmatrix}_{1\leq j\leq q+1}\\
\begin{pmatrix}b\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\frac{\kappa_{2,j-q-1,\ell}^{(S)}}{2}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial s_{\ell}^{2}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j-q-1}}p(t,\bm{s})\end{pmatrix}_{q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1+d)\times(q+1+d)}.

Furthermore, as
h,b,\frac{\max\{h,b\}^{4}}{h}\to 0
, and
nh^{3}b^{d}\to\infty
, we have that

\displaystyle\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})-\beta_{2}(t,\bm{s})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}O\left(h^{q+1}+b^{2}+\frac{b^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nh^{3}b^{d}}}\right)&\text{ if }q\text{ is odd and }(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}^{\circ},\\
O\left(h^{q}+b^{2}+\frac{b^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nh^{3}b^{d}}}\right)&\text{ if }q\text{ is even and }(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}^{\circ},\\
O\left(h^{q}+\frac{\max\{h,b\}^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nh^{3}b^{d}}}\right)&\text{ if }q\text{ is an integer and }(t,\bm{s})\in\partial\mathcal{E}.\end{cases}

Proof of Lemma
B.1
.

Recall from (
12
) that

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}(t,\bm{s}),\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}(t,\bm{s})\right)^{T}

\displaystyle=\left[\bm{X}^{T}(t,\bm{s})\bm{W}(t,\bm{s})\bm{X}(t,\bm{s})\right]^{-1}\bm{X}(t,\bm{s})^{T}\bm{W}(t,\bm{s})\bm{Y}

\displaystyle\equiv\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)^{-1}\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{Y}.

The proof of Lemma
B.1
has two major components, where we consider the cases when
(t,\bm{s})
is an interior point or a boundary point of the support
\mathcal{E}
. When
(t,\bm{s})
is an interior point of
\mathcal{E}
, we also divide the arguments into three steps that deal with the rates of convergence for the term
\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}
, the conditional covariance term
\mathrm{Cov}\left[\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}(t,\bm{s}),\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}(t,\bm{s})\right)^{T}\right]
, and the conditional bias term
\mathrm{Bias}\left[\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}(t,\bm{s}),\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}(t,\bm{s})\right)^{T}\Big|\mathbb{X}\right]
separately.

We first consider the case when
(t,\bm{s})
is an interior point of the support
\mathcal{E}
,
i.e.
, for any
(t_{1},\bm{s}_{1})\in\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{S}
,
\frac{t_{1}-t}{h}
and
\frac{\bm{s}_{1}-\bm{s}}{b}
lie in the supports of
K_{T}
and
K_{S}
respectively when
h,b
are small.
Notice that the conditional mean outcome function
\mu(t,\bm{s})=\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left(Y|T=t,\bm{S}=\bm{s}\right)
is well-defined in
\mathcal{E}
, and we have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}\left[\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}(t,\bm{s}),\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}(t,\bm{s})\right)^{T}\Big|\mathbb{X}\right]

\displaystyle=\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)^{-1}\bm{X}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{X}\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)^{-1}

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{Bias}\left[\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}(t,\bm{s}),\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}(t,\bm{s})\right)^{T}\Big|\mathbb{X}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}(t,\bm{s}),\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}(t,\bm{s})\right)^{T}\right]-\left(\bm{\beta}(t,\bm{s}),\bm{\alpha}(t,\bm{s})\right)^{T}

\displaystyle=\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)^{-1}\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\left[\begin{pmatrix}\mu(T_{1},\bm{S}_{1})\\
\vdots\\
\mu(T_{n},\bm{S}_{n})\end{pmatrix}-\bm{X}\begin{pmatrix}\bm{\beta}(t,\bm{s})\\
\bm{\alpha}(t,\bm{s})\end{pmatrix}\right],

where
\bm{\Sigma}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
is a diagonal matrix defined as:

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Var}\left(\bm{Y}|\mathbb{X}\right)\cdot\mathtt{Diag}\left(K_{T}^{2}\left(\frac{T_{1}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}^{2}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{1}-\bm{s}}{b}\right),...,K_{T}^{2}\left(\frac{T_{n}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}^{2}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{n}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\cdot\mathtt{Diag}\left(K_{T}^{2}\left(\frac{T_{1}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}^{2}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{1}-\bm{s}}{b}\right),...,K_{T}^{2}\left(\frac{T_{n}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}^{2}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{n}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)\right)

under Assumption
A3
with
\mathrm{Var}\left(\bm{Y}|\mathbb{X}\right)=\mathtt{Diag}\Big(\mathrm{Var}(Y_{1}|T_{1},\bm{S}_{1}),...,\mathrm{Var}(Y_{n}|T_{n},\bm{S}_{n})\Big)\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
. In addition, by Assumption
A3
and Taylor’s expansion, we know that

\left(\bm{\beta}(t,\bm{s}),\bm{\alpha}(t,\bm{s})\right)^{T}\equiv\left(\mu(t,\bm{s}),\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu(t,\bm{s}),...,\frac{1}{q!}\cdot\frac{\partial^{q}}{\partial t^{q}}\mu(t,\bm{s}),\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{1}}\mu(t,\bm{s}),...,\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{d}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right)^{T}.

Step 1: Common term
\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}
.
Before deriving the asymptotic behaviors of the above conditional covariance matrix and bias, we first study the rates of convergence of
\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1+d)\times(q+1+d)}
.

By direct calculations, we know that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}\\
&=\begin{cases}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}(T_{k}-t)^{i+j-2}K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right),\quad\quad 1\leq i,j\leq q+1,\\
\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}(T_{k}-t)^{i-1}\left(S_{k,j-q-1}-s_{j-q-1}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right),\quad 1\leq i\leq q+1\text{ and }q+1<j\leq q+1+d,\\
\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}\left(S_{k,i-q-1}-s_{i-q-1}\right)(T_{k}-t)^{j-1}K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right),\quad q+1<i\leq q+1+d\text{ and }1\leq j\leq q+1,\\
\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}\left(S_{k,i-q-1}-s_{i-q-1}\right)\left(S_{k,j-q-1}-s_{j-q-1}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right),\quad q+1<i,j\leq q+1+d.\end{cases}\end{split}

(27)

Here, each sample
\bm{S}_{k}\in\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is written as
\bm{S}_{k}=\left(S_{k,1},...,S_{k,d}\right)^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We now derive the asymptotic rates of convergence of the expectation and variance for each term in (
27
) under Assumptions
A3
,
A4
, and
A6
.

\bullet

Case I:

1\leq i,j\leq q+1
. We compute that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}\right]

\displaystyle=n\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\widetilde{t}-t\right)^{i+j-2}K_{T}\left(\frac{\widetilde{t}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{s}}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)p(\widetilde{t},\widetilde{\bm{s}})\,d\widetilde{t}d\widetilde{\bm{s}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{=}}nh^{i+j-1}b^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}u^{i+j-2}K_{T}(u)K_{S}(\bm{v})\cdot p(t+uh,\bm{s}+b\bm{v})\,dud\bm{v}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{=}}nh^{i+j-1}b^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}u^{i+j-2}K_{T}(u)K_{S}(\bm{v})\left[p(t,\bm{s})+uh\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})+b\bm{v}^{T}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{s}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)\right]dud\bm{v}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{=}}nh^{i+j-1}b^{d}\left[\kappa_{i+j-2}^{(T)}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})+h\cdot\kappa_{i+j-1}^{(T)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)\right],

where (i) utilizes the changes of variables
u=\frac{\widetilde{t}-t}{h}
and
\bm{v}=\frac{\widetilde{\bm{s}}-\bm{s}}{b}
, (ii) leverages the differentiability of
p(t,\bm{s})
and apply Taylor’s expansion, as well as (iii) uses the symmetric properties of
K_{T},K_{S}
with notations in Assumption
A6
(a). In addition, we calculate that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left[\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}\right]

\displaystyle=n\cdot\mathrm{Var}\left[(T_{1}-t)^{i+j-2}K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{1}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{1}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq n\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\widetilde{t}-t)^{2i+2j-4}K_{T}^{2}\left(\frac{\widetilde{t}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}^{2}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{s}}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)p(\widetilde{t},\widetilde{\bm{s}})\,d\widetilde{t}d\widetilde{\bm{s}}

\displaystyle=nh^{2i+2j-3}b^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}u^{2i+2j-4}K_{T}^{2}(u)K_{S}^{2}(v)p(t+uh,\bm{s}+b\bm{v})\,dud\bm{v}

\displaystyle=nh^{2i+2j-3}b^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}u^{2i+2j-4}K_{T}^{2}(u)K_{S}^{2}(v)\left[p(t,\bm{s})+uh\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})+b\bm{v}^{T}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{s}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)\right]dud\bm{v}

\displaystyle=nh^{2i+2j-3}b^{d}\left[\nu_{2i+2j-4}^{(T)}\nu_{0}^{(S)}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})+h\cdot\nu_{2i+2j-3}^{(T)}\nu_{0}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)\right].

The above calculations on
\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}\right]
and
\mathrm{Var}\left[\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}\right]
imply that

\displaystyle\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}\right]+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\left[\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}\right]}\right)

\displaystyle=nh^{i+j-1}b^{d}\left[\kappa_{i+j-2}^{(T)}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})+h\cdot\kappa_{i+j-1}^{(T)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]

for any
1\leq i,j\leq q+1
.

\bullet

Case II:

1\leq i\leq q+1
and
q+1<j\leq q+1+d
. We compute that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}\right]

\displaystyle=n\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\widetilde{t}-t\right)^{i-1}(\widetilde{s}_{j-q-1}-s_{j-q-1})K_{T}\left(\frac{\widetilde{t}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{s}}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)p(\widetilde{t},\widetilde{\bm{s}})\,d\widetilde{t}d\widetilde{\bm{s}}

\displaystyle=nh^{i}b^{d+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}u^{i-1}v_{j-q-1}K_{T}(u)K_{S}(\bm{v})p(t+hu,\bm{s}+b\bm{v})\,dud\bm{v}

\displaystyle=nh^{i}b^{d+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}u^{i-1}v_{j-q-1}K_{T}(u)K_{S}(\bm{v})\left[p(t,\bm{s})+uh\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})+b\bm{v}^{T}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{s}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)\right]dud\bm{v}

\displaystyle=nh^{i}b^{d+1}\left[b\cdot\kappa_{i-1}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,j-q-1}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j-q-1}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)\right]

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left[\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}\right]

\displaystyle=n\cdot\mathrm{Var}\left[(T_{1}-t)^{i-1}(S_{1,j-q-1}-s_{j-q-1})K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{1}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{1}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq n\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\widetilde{t}-t)^{2i-2}(\widetilde{s}_{j-q-1}-s_{j-q-1})^{2}K_{T}^{2}\left(\frac{\widetilde{t}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}^{2}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{s}}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)p(\widetilde{t},\widetilde{\bm{s}})\,d\widetilde{t}d\widetilde{\bm{s}}

\displaystyle=nh^{2i-1}b^{d+2}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}u^{2i-2}v_{j-q-1}^{2}K_{T}^{2}(u)K_{S}^{2}(\bm{v})\left[p(t,\bm{s})+uh\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})+b\bm{v}^{T}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{s}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)\right]dud\bm{v}

\displaystyle=nh^{2i-1}b^{d+2}\left[\nu_{2i-2}^{(T)}\nu_{2,j-q-1}^{(S)}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})+O(h)+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)\right].

These two terms indicate that

\displaystyle\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}\right]+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\left[\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}\right]}\right)

\displaystyle=nh^{i}b^{d+1}\left[b\cdot\kappa_{i-1}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,j-q-1}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j-q-1}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]

for any
1\leq i\leq q+1
and
q+1<j\leq q+1+d
.

\bullet

Case III:

q+1<i\leq q+1+d
and
1\leq j\leq q+1
. By swapping the roles of
i
and
j
in our calculations for
Case 2
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}

\displaystyle=nh^{j}b^{d+1}\left[b\cdot\kappa_{j-1}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,i-q-1}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{i-q-1}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]

for any
q+1<i\leq q+1+d
and
1\leq j\leq q+1
.

\bullet

Case IV:

q+1<i,j\leq q+1+d
. We compute that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}\right]

\displaystyle=n\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\widetilde{s}_{i-q-1}-s_{i-q-1})(\widetilde{s}_{j-q-1}-s_{j-q-1})K_{T}\left(\frac{\widetilde{t}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{s}}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)p(\widetilde{t},\widetilde{\bm{s}})\,d\widetilde{t}d\widetilde{\bm{s}}

\displaystyle=nhb^{d+2}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}v_{i-q-1}\cdot v_{j-q-1}K_{T}(u)K_{S}(\bm{v})\cdot p(t+uh,\bm{s}+b\bm{v})\,dud\bm{v}

\displaystyle=nhb^{d+2}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}v_{i-q-1}\cdot v_{j-q-1}K_{T}(u)K_{S}(\bm{v})\left[p(t,\bm{s})+uh\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})+b\bm{v}^{T}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{s}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)\right]dud\bm{v}

\displaystyle=nhb^{d+2}\left[\kappa_{2,i-q-1}^{(S)}\mathbbm{1}_{\{i=j\}}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)\right]

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left[\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}\right]

\displaystyle=n\cdot\mathrm{Var}\left[(S_{1,i-q-1}-s_{i-q-1})(S_{1,j-q-1}-s_{j-q-1})K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{1}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{1}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq n\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\widetilde{s}_{i-q-1}-s_{i-q-1})^{2}(\widetilde{s}_{j-q-1}-s_{j-q-1})^{2}K_{T}^{2}\left(\frac{\widetilde{t}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}^{2}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{s}}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)p(\widetilde{t},\widetilde{\bm{s}})\,d\widetilde{t}d\widetilde{\bm{s}}

\displaystyle=nhb^{d+4}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}v_{i-q-1}^{2}v_{j-q-1}^{2}K_{T}^{2}(u)K_{S}^{2}(\bm{v})\left[p(t,\bm{s})+uh\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})+b\bm{v}^{T}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{s}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)\right]dud\bm{v}

\displaystyle=nhb^{d+4}\left[\nu_{2,i-q-1}^{(S)}\nu_{2,j-q-1}^{(S)}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)\right].

The above calculations imply that

\displaystyle\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}\right]+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\left[\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}\right]}\right)

\displaystyle=nhb^{d+2}\left[\kappa_{2,i-q-1}^{(S)}\mathbbm{1}_{\{i=j\}}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]

for any
q+1<i,j\leq q+1+d
.

Therefore, we summarize all the above cases as:

\displaystyle\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}nh^{i+j-1}b^{d}\left[\kappa_{i+j-2}^{(T)}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})+h\cdot\kappa_{i+j-1}^{(T)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right],\\
\hskip 199.16928pt\quad 1\leq i,j\leq q+1,\\
nh^{i}b^{d+1}\left[b\cdot\kappa_{i-1}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,j-q-1}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j-q-1}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right],\\
\hskip 199.16928pt\quad 1\leq i\leq q+1\text{ and }q+1<j\leq q+1+d,\\
nh^{j}b^{d+1}\left[b\cdot\kappa_{j-1}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,i-q-1}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{i-q-1}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right],\\
\hskip 199.16928pt\quad q+1<i\leq q+1+d\text{ and }1\leq j\leq q+1,\\
nhb^{d+2}\left[\kappa_{2,i-q-1}^{(S)}\mathbbm{1}_{\{i=j\}}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right],\quad q+1<i,j\leq q+1+d.\end{cases}

Let
\bm{H}=\mathtt{Diag}(1,h,...,h^{q},b,...,b)\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1+d)\times(q+1+d)}
. We also denote

\bm{M}_{q}=\begin{pmatrix}\left(\kappa_{i+j-2}^{(T)}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq q+1}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}&\left(\kappa_{2,i-q-1}^{(S)}\mathbbm{1}_{\{i=j\}}\right)_{q+1<i,j\leq q+1+d}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1+d)\times(q+1+d)}

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{M}}_{q,h,b}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}h\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})\left(\kappa_{i+j-1}^{(T)}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq q+1}&b\cdot\mathtt{Diag}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{s}}p(t,\bm{s})\right)\left(\kappa_{i-1}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,j-q-1}^{(S)}\right)_{1\leq i\leq q+1,q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\\
b\cdot\mathtt{Diag}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{s}}p(t,\bm{s})\right)\left(\kappa_{j-1}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,i-q-1}^{(S)}\right)_{q+1<i\leq q+1+d,1\leq j\leq q+1}&\bm{0}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1+d)\times(q+1+d)}.

Then, we can rewrite the asymptotic behaviors of
\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}
in its matrix form as:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}=nhb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})\cdot\bm{H}\left[\bm{M}_{q}+\frac{\widetilde{\bm{M}}_{q,h,b}}{p(t,\bm{s})}+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\bm{H},\end{split}

(28)

where an abuse of notation is applied when we use
O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb}}\right)
to denote a matrix whose entries are of this order.
By the matrix inversion formula

(A+\max\{h,b\}\cdot B)^{-1}=A^{-1}-\max\{h,b\}\cdot A^{-1}BA^{-1}+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right),

we know that

\displaystyle\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{nhb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\cdot\bm{H}^{-1}\Bigg[\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}-\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\cdot\frac{\widetilde{\bm{M}}_{q,h,b}}{p(t,\bm{s})}\cdot\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\Bigg]\bm{H}^{-1}.

Step 2: Conditional covariance term
\mathrm{Cov}\left[\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}(t,\bm{s}),\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}(t,\bm{s})\right)^{T}\Big|\mathbb{X}\right]
.
Following our calculations for
\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}
for
1\leq i,j\leq q+1+d
, we can similarly derive that

\displaystyle\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\sigma^{2}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}(T_{k}-t)^{i+j-2}K_{T}^{2}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}^{2}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right),\quad\quad 1\leq i,j\leq q+1,\\
\sigma^{2}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}(T_{k}-t)^{i-1}\left(S_{k,j-q-1}-s_{j-q-1}\right)K_{T}^{2}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}^{2}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right),\quad 1\leq i\leq q+1\text{ and }q+1<j\leq q+1+d,\\
\sigma^{2}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}\left(S_{k,i-q-1}-s_{i-q-1}\right)(T_{k}-t)^{j-1}K_{T}^{2}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}^{2}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right),\quad q+1<i\leq q+1+d\text{ and }1\leq j\leq q+1,\\
\sigma^{2}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}\left(S_{k,i-q-1}-s_{i-q-1}\right)\left(S_{k,j-q-1}-s_{j-q-1}\right)K_{T}^{2}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}^{2}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right),\quad q+1<i,j\leq q+1+d,\end{cases}

and its asymptotic behaviors become

\displaystyle\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{X}\right)_{i,j}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\sigma^{2}nh^{i+j-1}b^{d}\left[\nu_{i+j-2}^{(T)}\nu_{0}^{(S)}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})+h\cdot\nu_{i+j-1}^{(T)}\nu_{0}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right],\\
\quad\hskip 284.52756pt1\leq i,j\leq q+1,\\
\sigma^{2}nh^{i}b^{d+1}\left[b\cdot\nu_{i-1}^{(T)}\nu_{2,j-q-1}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j-q-1}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right],\\
\quad\hskip 256.0748pt1\leq i\leq q+1\text{ and }q+1<j\leq q+1+d,\\
\sigma^{2}nh^{j}b^{d+1}\left[b\cdot\nu_{j-1}^{(T)}\nu_{2,i-q-1}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{i-q-1}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right],\\
\quad\hskip 256.0748ptq+1<i\leq q+1+d\text{ and }1\leq j\leq q+1,\\
\sigma^{2}nhb^{d+2}\left[\nu_{0}^{(T)}\nu_{2,i-q-1}^{(S)}\mathbbm{1}_{\{i=j\}}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right],\quad q+1<i,j\leq q+1+d.\end{cases}

If we denote

\bm{M}_{q}^{*}=\begin{pmatrix}\left(\nu_{i+j-2}^{(T)}\nu_{0}^{(S)}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq q+1}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}&\left(\nu_{0}^{(T)}\nu_{2,i-q-1}^{(S)}\mathbbm{1}_{\{i=j\}}\right)_{q+1<i,j\leq q+1+d}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1+d)\times(q+1+d)}

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{M}}_{q,h,b}^{*}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}h\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})\cdot\left(\nu_{i+j-1}^{(T)}\nu_{0}^{(S)}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq q+1}&b\cdot\mathtt{Diag}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{s}}p(t,\bm{s})\right)\left(\nu_{i-1}^{(T)}\nu_{2,j-q-1}^{(S)}\right)_{1\leq i\leq q+1,q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\\
b\cdot\mathtt{Diag}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{s}}p(t,\bm{s})\right)\left(\nu_{j-1}^{(T)}\nu_{2,i-q-1}^{(S)}\right)_{q+1<i\leq q+1+d,1\leq j\leq q+1}&\bm{0}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1+d)\times(q+1+d)},

then the asymptotic behavior of
\bm{X}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{X}
in its matrix form is

\displaystyle\bm{X}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{X}=nhb^{d}\sigma^{2}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})\bm{H}\left[\bm{M}_{q}^{*}+\frac{\widetilde{\bm{M}}_{q,h,b}^{*}}{p(t,\bm{s})}+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\bm{H}.

With our results for
\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}
in (
28
) and Assumption
A4
, we conclude that the asymptotic conditional covariance matrix of
\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}(t,\bm{s}),\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}(t,\bm{s})\right)^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{q+1+d}
is

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}\left[\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}(t,\bm{s}),\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}(t,\bm{s})\right)^{T}\Big|\mathbb{X}\right]

\displaystyle=\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)^{-1}\bm{X}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{X}\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{nhb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\cdot\bm{H}^{-1}\Bigg[\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}-\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\cdot\frac{\widetilde{\bm{M}}_{q,h,b}}{p(t,\bm{s})}\cdot\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[\bm{M}_{q}^{*}+\widetilde{\bm{M}}_{q,h,b}^{*}+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}-\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\cdot\frac{\widetilde{\bm{M}}_{q,h,b}}{p(t,\bm{s})}\cdot\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\bm{H}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{nhb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\cdot\bm{H}^{-1}\left[\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{M}_{q}^{*}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}+O\left(\max\{h,b\}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\bm{H}^{-1}.

In particular, we know that the asymptotic conditional variance of
\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})
is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\big|\mathbb{X}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{nh^{3}b^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\left[\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{M}_{q}^{*}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{e}_{2}+O\left(\max\{h,b\}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right],

where
\left\{\bm{e}_{1},...,\bm{e}_{q+1+d}\right\}
is the standard basis in
\mathbb{R}^{q+1+d}
. Since the only entries in
\bm{M}_{q},\bm{M}_{q}^{*}
that affects
\mathrm{Var}\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\big|\mathbb{X}\right]
are those in the first
(q+1+d)
rows and columns, the results follow by restricting
\bm{M}_{q},\bm{M}_{q}^{*}
to their first
(q+1)\times(q+1)
block matrices and taking
\bm{e}_{2}=(0,1,0,...,0)^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{q+1}
.

Step 3: Conditional bias term
\mathrm{Bias}\left[\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}(t,\bm{s}),\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}(t,\bm{s})\right)^{T}\Big|\mathbb{X}\right]
.
By Assumption
A3
and Taylor’s expansion, we have that

\mu(T,\bm{S})=\mu(t,\bm{s})+\sum_{j=1}^{q}\frac{1}{q!}\cdot\frac{\partial^{j}}{\partial t^{j}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\cdot(T-t)^{j}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{\ell}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\cdot(S_{\ell}-s_{\ell})+r(T,\bm{S}),

(29)

where the reminder term
r(T,\bm{S})
is given by

\displaystyle r(T,\bm{S})

\displaystyle=\frac{(T-t)^{q+1}}{(q+1)!}\left[\frac{\partial^{q+1}}{\partial t^{q+1}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]+\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t\partial s_{\ell}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right](T-t)(S_{\ell}-s_{\ell})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}(\bm{S}-\bm{s})^{T}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\bm{s}\partial\bm{s}^{T}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right](\bm{S}-\bm{s})+o\left(|T-t|^{q+1}+|T-t|\left|\left|\bm{S}-\bm{s}\right|\right|_{2}+\left|\left|\bm{S}-\bm{s}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right).

Thus, the conditional bias can be written as:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Bias}\left[\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}(t,\bm{s}),\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}(t,\bm{s})\right)^{T}\Big|\mathbb{X}\right]

\displaystyle=\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)^{-1}\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\left[\begin{pmatrix}\mu(T_{1},\bm{S}_{1})\\
\vdots\\
\mu(T_{n},\bm{S}_{n})\end{pmatrix}-\bm{X}\begin{pmatrix}\bm{\beta}(t,\bm{s})\\
\bm{\alpha}(t,\bm{s})\end{pmatrix}\right]

\displaystyle=\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)^{-1}\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\begin{pmatrix}r(T_{1},\bm{S}_{1})\\
\vdots\\
r(T_{n},\bm{S}_{n})\end{pmatrix}.

Now, we note that

\displaystyle\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\begin{pmatrix}r(T_{1},\bm{S}_{1})\\
\vdots\\
r(T_{n},\bm{S}_{n})\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}\left[\frac{\partial^{q+1}}{\partial t^{q+1}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\frac{(T_{k}-t)^{q+1}}{(q+1)!}\cdot K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)\\
+\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}\sum\limits_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t\partial s_{\ell}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right](T_{k}-t)\big(S_{k,\ell}-s_{\ell}\big)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)\\
+\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}(T_{k}-t)^{j-1}\cdot\frac{1}{2}(\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s})^{T}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\bm{s}\partial\bm{s}^{T}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right](\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s})K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)\end{pmatrix}_{1\leq j\leq q+1}\\
\begin{pmatrix}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}\left[\frac{\partial^{q+1}}{\partial t^{q+1}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\frac{(T_{k}-t)^{q+1}}{(q+1)!}\cdot(S_{k,j-q-1}-s_{j-q-1})\cdot K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)\\
+\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}\sum\limits_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t\partial s_{\ell}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right](T_{k}-t)(S_{k,\ell}-s_{\ell})(S_{k,j-q-1}-s_{j-q-1})K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)\\
+\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}(S_{k,j-q-1}-s_{j-q-1})\cdot\frac{1}{2}(\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s})^{T}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\bm{s}\partial\bm{s}^{T}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right](\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s})K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)\end{pmatrix}_{q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}nh^{q+1+j}b^{d}\left[\frac{\partial^{q+1}}{\partial t^{q+1}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\frac{1}{(q+1)!}\left[\kappa_{q+j}^{(T)}p(t,\bm{s})+\kappa_{q+j+1}^{(T)}h\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\\
+nh^{j+1}b^{d+1}\left[b\cdot\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t\partial s_{\ell}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\kappa_{j}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,\ell}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{\ell}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\\
+nh^{j}b^{d+2}\left[\kappa_{j-1}^{(T)}p(t,\bm{s})\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\frac{\kappa_{2,\ell}^{(S)}}{2}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial s_{\ell}^{2}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right)+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\end{pmatrix}_{1\leq j\leq q+1}\\
\begin{pmatrix}nh^{q+2}b^{d+1}\left[\frac{\partial^{q+1}}{\partial t^{q+1}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\frac{1}{(q+1)!}\left[b\kappa_{q+1}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,j-q-1}^{(S)}\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j-q-1}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\\
+nh^{2}b^{d+2}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t\partial s_{j-q-1}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\left[h\kappa_{2}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,j-q-1}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\\
+nhb^{d+3}\left[b\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\frac{\kappa_{2,j-q-1,\ell}^{(S)}}{2}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial s_{\ell}^{2}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j-q-1}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\end{pmatrix}_{q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=nhb^{d}\bm{H}\begin{bmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}h^{q+1}\left[\frac{\partial^{q+1}}{\partial t^{q+1}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\frac{1}{(q+1)!}\left[\kappa_{q+j}^{(T)}p(t,\bm{s})+\kappa_{q+j+1}^{(T)}h\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\\
+hb\left[b\cdot\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t\partial s_{\ell}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right)\kappa_{j}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,\ell}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{\ell}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\\
+b^{2}\left[\kappa_{j-1}^{(T)}p(t,\bm{s})\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\frac{\kappa_{2,\ell}^{(S)}}{2}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial s_{\ell}^{2}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right)+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\end{pmatrix}_{1\leq j\leq q+1}\\
\begin{pmatrix}h^{q+1}\left[\frac{\partial^{q+1}}{\partial t^{q+1}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\frac{1}{(q+1)!}\left[b\kappa_{q+1}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,j-q-1}^{(S)}\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j-q-1}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\\
+hb\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t\partial s_{j-q-1}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\left[h\kappa_{2}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,j-q-1}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\\
+b^{2}\left[b\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\frac{\kappa_{2,j-q-1,\ell}^{(S)}}{2}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial s_{\ell}^{2}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j-q-1}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\end{pmatrix}_{q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\end{bmatrix}.

Therefore, we know from the above display and (
28
) that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Bias}\left[\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}(t,\bm{s}),\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}(t,\bm{s})\right)^{T}\Big|\mathbb{X}\right]

\displaystyle=\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)^{-1}\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\begin{pmatrix}r(T_{1},\bm{S}_{1})\\
\vdots\\
r(T_{n},\bm{S}_{n})\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p(t,\bm{s})}\cdot\bm{H}^{-1}\Bigg[\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}+O\left(\max\{h,b\}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad\times\begin{bmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}h^{q+1}\left[\frac{\partial^{q+1}}{\partial t^{q+1}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\frac{1}{(q+1)!}\left[\kappa_{q+j}^{(T)}p(t,\bm{s})+\kappa_{q+j+1}^{(T)}h\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\\
+hb\left[b\cdot\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t\partial s_{\ell}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\kappa_{j}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,\ell}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{\ell}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\\
+b^{2}\left[\kappa_{j-1}^{(T)}p(t,\bm{s})\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\frac{\kappa_{2,\ell}^{(S)}}{2}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial s_{\ell}^{2}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right)+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\end{pmatrix}_{1\leq j\leq q+1}\\
\begin{pmatrix}h^{q+1}\left[\frac{\partial^{q+1}}{\partial t^{q+1}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\frac{1}{(q+1)!}\left[b\kappa_{q+1}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,j-q-1}^{(S)}\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j-q-1}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\\
+hb\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t\partial s_{j-q-1}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\left[h\kappa_{2}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,j-q-1}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\\
+b^{2}\left[b\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\frac{\kappa_{2,j-q-1,\ell}^{(S)}}{2}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial s_{\ell}^{2}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j-q-1}}p(t,\bm{s})+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\end{pmatrix}_{q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p(t,\bm{s})}\bm{H}^{-1}\left[\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}+O\left(\max\{h,b\}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[h^{q+1}\bm{\tau}_{q}+hb\bm{\tau}_{q}^{*}+b^{2}\widetilde{\bm{\tau}}_{q}+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{4}\right)+o_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right],

where

\displaystyle\bm{\tau}_{q}=\frac{\left[\frac{\partial^{q+1}}{\partial t^{q+1}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]}{(q+1)!}\begin{bmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{q+j}^{(T)}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})+h\cdot\kappa_{q+j+1}^{(T)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})\end{pmatrix}_{1\leq j\leq q+1}\\
\begin{pmatrix}b\kappa_{q+1}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,j-q-1}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j-q-1}}p(t,\bm{s})\end{pmatrix}_{q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1+d)\times(q+1+d)},

\displaystyle\bm{\tau}_{q}^{*}=\begin{bmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}b\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t\partial s_{\ell}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\kappa_{j}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,\ell}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{\ell}}p(t,\bm{s})\end{pmatrix}_{1\leq j\leq q+1}\\
\begin{pmatrix}h\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t\partial s_{j-q-1}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\kappa_{2}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,j-q-1}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})\end{pmatrix}_{q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1+d)\times(q+1+d)},

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{\tau}}_{q}=\begin{bmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{j-1}^{(T)}p(t,\bm{s})\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\frac{\kappa_{2,\ell}^{(S)}}{2}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial s_{\ell}^{2}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right)\end{pmatrix}_{1\leq j\leq q+1}\\
\begin{pmatrix}b\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\frac{\kappa_{2,j-q-1,\ell}^{(S)}}{2}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial s_{\ell}^{2}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j-q-1}}p(t,\bm{s})\end{pmatrix}_{q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1+d)\times(q+1+d)}.

Since
\kappa_{2j-1}^{(T)}=0
for all
j=1,2,...,q+1
by Assumption
A6
(a), we know that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bm{M}_{q}&=\begin{pmatrix}\left(\kappa_{i+j-2}^{(T)}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq q+1}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}&\left(\kappa_{2,i-q-1}^{(S)}\mathbbm{1}_{\{i=j\}}\right)_{q+1<i,j\leq q+1+d}\end{pmatrix}\\
&=\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{0}^{(T)}&0&\kappa_{2}^{(T)}&0&\cdots&\kappa_{q}^{(T)}&\\
0&\kappa_{2}^{(T)}&0&\kappa_{4}^{(T)}&\cdots&\kappa_{q+1}^{(T)}&\\
\vdots&\lx@intercol\hfil\ddots\hfil\lx@intercol&\vdots&\bm{0}\\
\kappa_{q}^{(T)}&\lx@intercol\hfil\cdots\hfil\lx@intercol&\kappa_{2q}^{(T)}&\\
&&\bm{0}&&&&\left(\kappa_{2,i-q-1}^{(S)}\mathbbm{1}_{\{i=j\}}\right)_{q+1<i,j\leq q+1+d}\end{pmatrix},\end{split}

(30)

i.e.
, each row/column of the upper
(q+1)\times(q+1)
submatrix of
M_{q}
has interleaving nonzero and zero entries. More importantly, given that

\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}=\frac{1}{\det(\bm{M}_{q})}\cdot\mathrm{adj}(\bm{M}_{q})

with
\mathrm{adj}(\bm{M}_{q})\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1+d)\times(q+1+d)}
being the adjugate of
\bm{M}_{q}
, it can be shown that
\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}
has the identical sparsity structures as
\bm{M}_{q}
. In more details, any
(i,j)
-minor of
\bm{M}_{q}
with
i+j
being odd has two rows/columns that share an identical sparsity structure. Recalling the formula of calculating a determinant

\det(A)=\sum_{\sigma_{q}}\left(\text{sign}(\sigma_{q})\prod_{i=1}^{q+1+d}A_{i\sigma_{q}(i)}\right),

where the sum is over all
(q+1+d)!
permutations
\sigma_{q}
, we know that each summand is zero when we compute the determinant of
(i,j)
-minor of
\bm{M}_{q}
with
i+j
being odd through the above formula. Hence, the determinant of
(i,j)
-minor of
\bm{M}_{q}
with
i+j
being odd is zero. A similar argument can be found in the proof of Theorem 2 in
Fan et al. (1996)
.

Using this result, we derive the bias term
\mathrm{Bias}\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\big|\mathbb{X}\right]
when
q
is either odd or even (recommended) as follows.

\bullet

Case I:
q
is odd.
Then,

\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}=\left(\underbrace{0,\star,0,\star,...,0,\star}_{1\leq j\leq q+1},\underbrace{\bm{0}}_{q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{q+1+d},

where
\star\in\mathbb{R}
stands for some nonzero bounded value (not necessarily equal for each entry). This implies that

\displaystyle\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\tau}_{q}

\displaystyle=\left(\underbrace{0,\star,0,\star,...,0,\star}_{1\leq j\leq q+1},\underbrace{\bm{0}}_{q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\right)\frac{\left[\frac{\partial^{q+1}}{\partial t^{q+1}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]}{(q+1)!}\begin{bmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{q+1}^{(T)}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})\\
h\cdot\kappa_{q+3}^{(T)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})\\
\vdots\\
h\cdot\kappa_{2q+2}^{(T)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})\end{pmatrix}_{1\leq j\leq q+1}\\
\begin{pmatrix}b\cdot\kappa_{q+1}^{(T)}\kappa_{2,j-q-1}^{(S)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{j-q-1}}p(t,\bm{s})\end{pmatrix}_{q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=h\cdot\tau_{q}^{\text{odd}},

where
\tau_{q}^{\text{odd}}=\frac{\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\tau}_{q}}{h}
is a dominating term independent of
h,b
. Similarly, we know that

\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\tau}_{q}^{*}=b\cdot\tau_{q}^{*},\quad\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}=0,

where
\tau_{q}^{*}=\frac{\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\tau}_{q}^{*}}{b}
is a constant independent of
b,h
. Hence, when
q
is odd and
h,b\to 0,nhb^{d}\to\infty
, we obtain that the asymptotic conditional bias of
\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})
is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathrm{Bias}\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\big|\mathbb{X}\right]&=\frac{1}{h\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\left[h^{q+1}\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\tau}_{q}+hb\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\tau}_{q}^{*}+b^{2}\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\widetilde{\bm{\tau}}_{q}+o_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\\
&=\frac{1}{p(t,\bm{s})}\left[h^{q+1}\tau_{q}^{\text{odd}}+b^{2}\tau_{q}^{*}+O\left(\frac{b^{4}}{h}\right)+o_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right].\end{split}

(31)

\bullet

Case II:
q
is even.
Then,

\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}=\left(\underbrace{0,\star,0,\star,...,0,\star,0}_{1\leq j\leq q+1},\underbrace{\bm{0}}_{q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{q+1+d},

where
\star\in\mathbb{R}
stands for some nonzero bounded value (not necessarily equal for each entry). This indicates that

\displaystyle\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\tau}_{q}

\displaystyle=\left(\underbrace{0,\star,0,\star,...,0,\star,0}_{1\leq j\leq q+1},\underbrace{\bm{0}}_{q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\right)\left[\frac{\partial^{q+1}}{\partial t^{q+1}}\mu(t,\bm{s})\right]\frac{1}{(q+1)!}\begin{bmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}h\cdot\kappa_{q+2}^{(T)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})\\
\kappa_{q+2}^{(T)}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})\\
\vdots\\
h\cdot\kappa_{2q+2}^{(T)}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(t,\bm{s})\end{pmatrix}_{1\leq j\leq q+1}\\
\bm{0}_{q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\tau_{q}^{\text{even}},

where
\tau_{q}^{\text{even}}=\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\tau}_{q}
is a dominating constant independent of
h,b
. Similarly, we know that

\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\tau}_{q}^{*}=b\cdot\tau_{q}^{*},\quad\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}=0,

where
\tau_{q}^{*}=\frac{\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\tau}_{q}^{*}}{b}
is a constant independent of
b,h
. Hence, when
q
is even and
h,b\to 0,nhb^{d}\to\infty
, we obtain that the asymptotic conditional bias of
\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})
is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Bias}\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\big|\mathbb{X}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\left[h^{q+1}\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\tau}_{q}+hb\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\tau}_{q}^{*}+b^{2}\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\widetilde{\bm{\tau}}_{q}+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{4}\right)+o_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p(t,\bm{s})}\left[h^{q}\tau_{q}^{\text{even}}+b^{2}\tau_{q}^{*}+O\left(\frac{b^{4}}{h}\right)+o_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right].

In summary, as
h,b\to 0
and
nh^{3}b^{d}\to\infty
, we know that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Bias}\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\big|\mathbb{X}\right]=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{p(t,\bm{s})}\left[h^{q+1}\tau_{q}^{\text{odd}}+b^{2}\tau_{q}^{*}+O\left(\frac{b^{4}}{h}\right)+o_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]&q\text{ is odd},\\
\frac{1}{p(t,\bm{s})}\left[h^{q}\tau_{q}^{\text{even}}+b^{2}\tau_{q}^{*}+O\left(\frac{b^{4}}{h}\right)+o_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]&q\text{ is even}.\end{cases}

Now, we consider the case when
(t,\bm{s})
lies in the boundary region of
\mathcal{E}
. In this case, those dominating constants in
\mathrm{Var}\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\big|\mathbb{X}\right]
and
\mathrm{Bias}\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\big|\mathbb{X}\right]
would be different but the asymptotic rates remain the same due to Assumption
A5
(a). In more details,
\kappa_{j}^{(T)},\nu_{j}^{(T)},\kappa_{j,\ell}^{(S)},\nu_{j,k}^{(S)}
are now defined as:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\kappa_{j}^{(T)}=\int_{\left\{u\in\mathbb{R}:(t+hu,\bm{s}+b\bm{v})\in\mathcal{E}\text{ for some }\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\right\}}u^{j}K_{T}(u)\,du<\infty,\\
&\nu_{j}^{(T)}=\int_{\left\{u\in\mathbb{R}:(t+hu,\bm{s}+b\bm{v})\in\mathcal{E}\text{ for some }\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\right\}}u^{j}K_{T}^{2}(u)\,du<\infty,\\
&\kappa_{j,\ell}^{(S)}=\int_{\left\{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:(t+hu,\bm{s}+b\bm{v})\in\mathcal{E}\text{ for some }u\in\mathbb{R}\right\}}u_{\ell}^{j}K_{S}(\bm{u})\,d\bm{u}<\infty,\\
&\text{ and }\quad\nu_{j,k}^{(S)}=\int_{\left\{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:(t+hu,\bm{s}+b\bm{v})\in\mathcal{E}\text{ for some }u\in\mathbb{R}\right\}}u_{\ell}^{j}K_{S}^{2}(\bm{u})\,d\bm{u}<\infty.\end{split}

(32)

These terms are again bounded even when
h,b\to 0
. Under the above new definitions, we can redefine
\bm{M}_{q},\widetilde{\bm{M}}_{q,h,b},\bm{M}_{q}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{M}}_{q,h,b}^{*},\bm{\tau}_{q},\bm{\tau}_{q}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{\tau}}_{q}
accordingly, where those partial derivatives of
p(t,\bm{s})
and the corresponding Taylor’s expansion are defined at a nearby interior point by Assumption
A5
(a) and taking the limit to the boundary
\partial\mathcal{E}
. More importantly, the matrix
\bm{M}_{q}
remains non-singular as
h,b\to 0
due to Assumption
A5
(a) and Lemma 7.1 in
Fan and Guerre (2015)
. A similar argument can also be found in Theorem 2.2 in
Ruppert and Wand (1994)
.

However, the matrices
\bm{M}_{q}
and
\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}
no longer have the interleaving zero structures as in (
30
) because of the asymmetric integrated ranges for those terms in (
32
). Nevertheless, by our Assumption
A5
(b), we know that both
\bm{\tau}_{q}^{*}=\widetilde{\bm{\tau}}_{q}=\bm{0}
. Thus, no matter
q>0
is odd or even, the asymptotic conditional bias of
\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})
is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Bias}\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\big|\mathbb{X}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\left[h^{q+1}\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\tau}_{q}+hb\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\tau}_{q}^{*}+b^{2}\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\widetilde{\bm{\tau}}_{q}+O\left(\max\{h,b\}^{4}\right)+o_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p(t,\bm{s})}\left[h^{q}\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\tau}_{q}+O\left(\frac{\max\{h,b\}^{4}}{h}\right)+o_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right].

Finally, for any
(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}
, the unconditional asymptotic rate of convergence of
\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})
can be derived by noting that

\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left(\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\big|\mathbb{X}\right)\right]\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathrm{Var}\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{Var}\left(\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\big|\mathbb{X}\right)\right]+\mathrm{Var}\left[\mathbb{E}\left(\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\big|\mathbb{X}\right)\right].

Therefore, if
(t,\bm{s})
is an interior point of
\mathcal{E}
, then we have that

\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})-\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu(t,\bm{s})=\begin{cases}O\left(h^{q+1}+b^{2}+\frac{b^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nh^{3}b^{d}}}\right)&q\text{ is odd},\\
O\left(h^{q}+b^{2}+\frac{b^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nh^{3}b^{d}}}\right)&q\text{ is even},\end{cases}

as
h,b\to 0
and
nh^{3}b^{d}\to\infty
. Otherwise, if
(t,\bm{s})\in\partial\mathcal{E}
, then we have that

\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})-\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu(t,\bm{s})=O\left(h^{q}+\frac{\max\{h,b\}^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nh^{3}b^{d}}}\right)

as
h,b\to 0
and
nh^{3}b^{d}\to\infty
. The results follow.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs of Results in the Main Paper

This section consists of the proofs for theorems, lemmas, and propositions in the main paper as well as other auxiliary results.

C.1
Proof of Proposition
2

Proposition 2

(Properties of the additive confounding model)
.

Let
\mu(t,\bm{s})=\mathbb{E}\left(Y|T=t,\bm{S}=\bm{s}\right)=\bar{m}(t)+\eta(\bm{s})
under the additive confounding model (
7
). Assume that
\bar{m}(t)
is differentiable for any
t\in\mathcal{T}
. The following results hold for any
t\in\mathcal{T}
under Assumption
A1
:

(a)

\mathbb{E}\left[\mu(t,\bm{S})\right]=\bar{m}(t)
when
\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[\eta(\bm{S})\right]=0
.

(b)

\theta(t)\neq\frac{d}{dt}\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[\mu(t,\bm{S})|T=t\right]
when
\frac{d}{dt}\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[\eta(\bm{S})|T=t\right]\neq 0
.

(c)

\theta(t)=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu(t,\bm{S})\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu(t,\bm{S})\big|T=t\right]=\theta_{C}(t)
.

(d)

\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}(Y)=\mathbb{E}\left[\mu(T,\bm{S})\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[m(T)\right]
, where
m(t)=\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[Y(t)\right]
.

Proof of Proposition
2
.

All the results follow from some simple calculations and the fact that

\mu(t,\bm{s})=\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left(Y|T=t,\bm{S}=\bm{s}\right)=\bar{m}(t)+\eta(\bm{s})

under the additive confounding model (
7
).

(a) Given that
\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[\eta(\bm{S})\right]=0
, we have that

\displaystyle\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[\mu(t,\bm{S})\right]=\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left(Y|T=t,\bm{S}\right)\right]=\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[\bar{m}(t)+\eta(\bm{S})\right]=\bar{m}(t).

(b) First, we notice that
m(t)=\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[Y(t)\right]=\bar{m}(t)+\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[\eta(\bm{S})\right]
and
\theta(t)=\bar{m}^{\prime}(t)
under model (
7
). Then,

\frac{d}{dt}\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[\mu(t,\bm{S})|T=t\right]=\frac{d}{dt}\left\{\bar{m}(t)+\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[\eta(\bm{S})|T=t\right]\right\}=\bar{m}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{d}{dt}\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left(\eta(\bm{S})|T=t\right)\neq\theta(t)

when
\frac{d}{dt}\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[\eta(\bm{S})|T=t\right]\neq 0
.

(c) From (b), we know that

\displaystyle\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu(t,\bm{S})\right]=\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[\bar{m}^{\prime}(t)\right]=\bar{m}^{\prime}(t)=\theta(t).

In addition, we also have that

\displaystyle\theta_{C}(t)

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu(t,\bm{S})\Big|T=t\right]=\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[\bar{m}^{\prime}(t)|T=t\right]=\bar{m}^{\prime}(t)=\theta(t).

(d) On one hand, we know that

\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}(Y)=\mathbb{E}\left[\mu(T,\bm{S})\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\bar{m}(T)+\eta(\bm{S})\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\bar{m}(T)\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\eta(\bm{S})\right].

On the other hand, we also have that

\mathbb{E}\left[m(T)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left\{\bar{m}(T)+\mathbb{E}\left[\eta(\bm{S})\right]\right\}=\mathbb{E}\left[\bar{m}(T)\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\eta(\bm{S})\right].

The proof is thus completed.
∎

C.2
Proof of Lemma
3

Lemma 3

(Uniform convergence of
\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})
)
.

Let
q>0
. Suppose that Assumptions
A3
,
A4
,
A5
, and
A6
(a,b) hold. Let
\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})
be the second element of
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}(t,\bm{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{q+1}
and
\beta_{2}(t,\bm{s})=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu(t,\bm{s})
. Then, as
h,b,\frac{\max\{h,b\}^{4}}{h}\to 0
and
\frac{nh^{3}b^{d}}{|\log(hb^{d})|},\frac{|\log(hb^{d})|}{\log\log n}\to\infty
, we know that

\sup_{(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}}\left|\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})-\beta_{2}(t,\bm{s})\right|=O\left(h^{q}\right)+O\left(b^{2}\right)+O\left(\frac{\max\{h,b\}^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log(hb^{d})|}{nh^{3}b^{d}}}\right).

Proof of Lemma
3
.

The proof of Lemma
3
is partially inspired by the proof of Lemma 7 in
Cheng and Chen (2019)
. Recall from (
12
) that

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}(t,\bm{s}),\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}(t,\bm{s})\right)^{T}

\displaystyle=\left[\bm{X}^{T}(t,\bm{s})\bm{W}(t,\bm{s})\bm{X}(t,\bm{s})\right]^{-1}\bm{X}(t,\bm{s})^{T}\bm{W}(t,\bm{s})\bm{Y}\equiv\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)^{-1}\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{Y}.

By direct calculations with
\bm{H}=\mathtt{Diag}(1,h,...,h^{q},b,...,b)\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1+d)\times(q+1+d)}
, we have that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}(t,\bm{s}),\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}(t,\bm{s})\right)^{T}&=\left(\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{X}\right)^{-1}\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{Y}\\
&=\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}\cdot\bm{H}^{-1}\left[\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})^{T}\bm{W}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})\right]^{-1}\bm{H}^{-1}\bm{X}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{Y}\\
&=\bm{H}^{-1}\left[\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})^{T}\bm{W}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})\right]^{-1}\left[\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})^{T}\bm{W}\bm{Y}\right].\end{split}

(33)

We first derive the uniform rate of convergence for
\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})^{T}\bm{W}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})
. Recall from (
27
) in the proof of Lemma
B.1
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left[\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})^{T}\bm{W}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})\right]_{i,j}\\
&=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)^{i+j-2}K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right),\quad\quad 1\leq i,j\leq q+1,\\
\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)^{i-1}\left(\frac{S_{k,j-q-1}-s_{j-q-1}}{b}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right),\quad 1\leq i\leq q+1\text{ and }q+1<j\leq q+1+d,\\
\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}\left(\frac{S_{k,i-q-1}-s_{i-q-1}}{b}\right)\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)^{j-1}K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right),\quad q+1<i\leq q+1+d\text{ and }1\leq j\leq q+1,\\
\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}\left(\frac{S_{k,i-q-1}-s_{i-q-1}}{b}\right)\left(\frac{S_{k,j-q-1}-s_{j-q-1}}{b}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right),\quad q+1<i,j\leq q+1+d.\end{cases}\end{split}

(34)

By our notation in (
25
), we can obtain from (
28
) in the proof of Lemma
B.1
that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})^{T}\bm{W}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})

\displaystyle=p(t,\bm{s})\bm{M}_{q}+O\left(\max\{h,b\}\right)+\underbrace{\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})^{T}\bm{W}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})^{T}\bm{W}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})\right]}_{\textbf{Term A}},

where we apply an abuse of notation when using
O\left(\max\{h,b\}\right)
to denote a matrix whose entries are of this order.

By (
34
), each entry of the matrix in
Term A
is a mean-zero empirical process (scaled by
\frac{1}{\sqrt{nhb^{d}}}
) over a function in the class
\mathcal{K}_{q,d}
defined in Assumption
A6
(b). By Theorem 2.3 in
Giné and Guillou (2002)
or Theorem 1 in
Einmahl and Mason (2005)
, we know that

\sup_{(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}}\left|\left|\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})^{T}\bm{W}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})^{T}\bm{W}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})\right]\right|\right|_{\max}=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log(hb^{d})|}{nhb^{d}}}\right),

where
\left|\left|\bm{A}\right|\right|_{\max}=\max_{i,j}|\bm{A}_{ij}|
for
\bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1+d)\times(q+1+d)}
. Therefore, we conclude that

\sup_{(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}}\left|\left|\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})^{T}\bm{W}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})-p(t,\bm{s})\bm{M}_{q}\right|\right|_{\max}=O\left(\max\{h,b\}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log(hb^{d})|}{nhb^{d}}}\right)

and

\sup_{(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}}\left|\left|\left[\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}\left(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1}\right)^{T}\bm{W}\left(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1}\right)\right]^{-1}-\frac{\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}}{p(t,\bm{s})}\right|\right|_{\max}=O\left(\max\{h,b\}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log(hb^{d})|}{nhb^{d}}}\right).

Based on the above result and (
33
), we have that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})&=\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}(t,\bm{s})\\
\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}(t,\bm{s})\end{bmatrix}\\
&=\frac{1}{h}\cdot\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\left[\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})^{T}\bm{W}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})\right]^{-1}\left[\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})^{T}\bm{W}\bm{Y}\right]\\
&=\frac{1}{h\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\cdot\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\left[\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}+\bm{A}_{h,b}\right]\left[\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})^{T}\bm{W}\bm{Y}\right],\end{split}

(35)

where each entry of
\bm{A}_{h,b}\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1+d)\times(q+1+d)}
is uniformly of the order
O\left(\max\{h,b\}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log(hb^{d})|}{nhb^{d}}}\right)
. Notice also that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}(\bm{X}\bm{H}^{-1})^{T}\bm{W}\bm{Y}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\left(\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}Y_{k}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)^{j-1}K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)\right)_{1\leq j\leq q+1}\\
\left(\frac{1}{nhb^{d}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}Y_{k}\left(\frac{S_{k,j-q-1}-s_{j-q-1}}{b}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{k}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)\right)_{q+1<j\leq q+1+d}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right),

where
\mathbb{P}_{n}
is the empirical measure associated with observations
\left\{(Y_{k},T_{k},\bm{S}_{k})\right\}_{k=1}^{n}
and
\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}(y,z,\bm{v})=y\cdot\bm{\psi}_{t,\bm{s}}(z,\bm{v})
defined in (
26
).
Plugging this result back into (
35
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})&=\frac{1}{h}\cdot\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\left[\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}+\bm{A}_{h,b}\right]\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right)\\
&=\frac{1}{h\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\cdot\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}}\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right)+\frac{1}{h\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\cdot\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}}\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{A}_{h,b}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right).\end{split}

(36)

Equivalently, we can scale and center
\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})
as:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sqrt{nh^{3}b^{d}}\left\{\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})-\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\right]\right\}\\
&=\sqrt{nhb^{d}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right)-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right)\right]\\
&\quad+\sqrt{nhb^{d}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{A}_{h,b}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right)-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{A}_{h,b}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right)\right]\\
&=\sqrt{hb^{d}}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right)+\sqrt{hb^{d}}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{A}_{h,b}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right),\end{split}

(37)

where
\mathbb{G}_{n}f=\sqrt{n}\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\right)f=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left\{f(Y_{k},T_{k},\bm{S}_{k})-\mathbb{E}\left[f(Y_{k},T_{k},\bm{S}_{k})\right]\right\}
. One auxiliary result that we derive here is a form of the uniform Bahadur representation
(
Bahadur, 1966
;
Kong et al., 2010
)
as:

\displaystyle\sup_{(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}}\left|\frac{\sqrt{nh^{3}b^{d}}\left\{\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})-\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\right]\right\}-\sqrt{hb^{d}}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right)}{\sqrt{hb^{d}}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right)}\right|

\displaystyle=O\left(\max\{h,b\}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log(hb^{d})|}{nhb^{d}}}\right),

because each entry of
\bm{A}_{h,b}\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1+d)\times(q+1+d)}
is uniformly of the order
O\left(\max\{h,b\}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log(hb^{d})|}{nhb^{d}}}\right)
.

We remain to derive the uniform rate of convergence for
\sqrt{hb^{d}}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\cdot\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right)
and
\sqrt{hb^{d}}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\cdot\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right)
. Recall from our notation in (
26
) that

\displaystyle\sqrt{hb^{d}}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\cdot\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{nhb^{d}}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left\{\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}(Y_{k},T_{k},\bm{S}_{k})-\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}(Y_{k},T_{k},\bm{S}_{k})\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{nhb^{d}}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left\{Y_{k}\cdot\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\psi}_{t,\bm{s}}(T_{k},\bm{S}_{k})-\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{k}\cdot\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\psi}_{t,\bm{s}}(T_{k},\bm{S}_{k})\right]\right\}.

Since each entry of
(z,\bm{v})\mapsto\bm{\psi}_{t,\bm{s}}(z,\bm{v})
is simply a linear combination of functions in
\mathcal{K}_{q,d}
defined in Assumption
A6
(b), we can apply Theorem 4 in
Einmahl and Mason (2005)
to establish that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}}\left|\sqrt{hb^{d}}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\cdot\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right)\right|\\
&=\sup_{(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{nhb^{d}}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left\{Y_{k}\cdot\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\psi}_{t,\bm{s}}(T_{k},\bm{S}_{k})-\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{k}\cdot\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\psi}_{t,\bm{s}}(T_{k},\bm{S}_{k})\right]\right\}\right|\\
&=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{|\log(hb^{d})|}\right)\end{split}

(38)

when
\frac{|\log(hb^{d})|}{\log\log n}\to\infty
. Similarly, we have that

\sup_{(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}}\left|\sqrt{hb^{d}}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\cdot\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right)\right|=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{|\log(hb^{d})|}\right).

Plugging these two rates of convergence back into (
37
), we obtain that as
h,b\to 0
and
\frac{nh^{3}b^{d}}{|\log(hb^{d})|}\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}}\left|\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})-\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\right]\right|\\
&=\sup_{(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}}\left|\frac{\sqrt{hb^{d}}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\cdot\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right)+\sqrt{hb^{d}}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\cdot\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{A}_{h,b}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right)}{\sqrt{nh^{3}b^{d}}}\right|\\
