&\leq\sup_{(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}}\left|\frac{\sqrt{hb^{d}}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\cdot\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right)}{\sqrt{nh^{3}b^{d}}}\right|+\sup_{(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}}\left|\frac{\sqrt{hb^{d}}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\frac{1}{hb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\cdot\bm{e}_{2}^{T}\bm{A}_{h,b}\bm{\Psi}_{t,\bm{s}}\right)}{\sqrt{nh^{3}b^{d}}}\right|\\
&=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log(hb^{d})|}{nh^{3}b^{d}}}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log(hb^{d})|}{nh^{3}b^{d}}}\right)\left[O\left(\max\{h,b\}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log(hb^{d})|}{nhb^{d}}}\right)\right]\\
&=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log(hb^{d})|}{nh^{3}b^{d}}}\right).\end{split}

(39)

Together with the rate of convergence for the bias term
\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})\right]-\beta_{2}(t,\bm{s})
in Lemma
B.1
, we conclude that

\sup_{(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}}\left|\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})-\beta_{2}(t,\bm{s})\right|=O\left(h^{q}+b^{2}+\frac{\max\{b,h\}^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log(hb^{d})|}{nh^{3}b^{d}}}\right),

where we combine the odd and even cases for
q>0
by arguing that
O(h^{q+1})
is dominated by
O(h^{q})
when
h
is small. The proof is thus completed.
∎

C.3
Proof of
Theorem 4

Theorem 4

(Convergence of
\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)
and
\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)
)
.

Let
q>0
and
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{T}
be a compact set so that
p_{T}(t)
is uniformly bounded away from 0 within
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
. Suppose that Assumptions
A1
,
A2
,
A3
,
A4
,
A5
, and
A6
hold. Then, as
h,b,\hslash,\frac{\max\{b,h\}^{4}}{h}\to 0
, and
\frac{n\max\{h,\hslash\}b^{d}}{|\log(\hslash hb^{d})|},\frac{|\log(h\hslash b^{d})|}{\log\log n},\frac{nh^{3}}{|\log h|}\to\infty
, we know that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)-\theta(t)\right|

\displaystyle=O\left(h^{q}+b^{2}+\frac{\max\{b,h\}^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{3}}}+\hslash^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{|\log\hslash|}{n\hslash}}\right)

and

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)\right|

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)+O\left(h^{q}+b^{2}+\frac{\max\{b,h\}^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{3}}}+\hslash^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{|\log\hslash|}{n\hslash}}\right).

Proof of
Theorem 4
.

Assume, without loss of generality, that the set
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
is connected. Otherwise, we focus our analysis on a connected component of
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
and take the union afterwards.

Recall from (
10
) and (
16
) that the integral estimator is defined as:

\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[Y_{i}+\int_{\widetilde{t}=T_{i}}^{\widetilde{t}=t}\widehat{\theta}_{C}(\widetilde{t})\,d\widetilde{t}\right]=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}+\widehat{\Delta}_{h,b}(t).

Moreover, under Assumption
A2
,

m(t)=\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[m(T)\right]+\Delta(t)=\mathbb{E}(Y)+\Delta(t),

where we define

\Delta(t)\equiv\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}\left[\int_{\widetilde{t}=T}^{\widetilde{t}=t}\theta(\widetilde{t})d\widetilde{t}\right]=\int\int_{\widetilde{t}=\tau}^{\widetilde{t}=t}\theta(\widetilde{t})\,d\widetilde{t}\,d\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}_{T}(\tau)=\int\int_{\widetilde{t}=\tau}^{\widetilde{t}=t}\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})\,d\widetilde{t}\,d\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}_{T}(\tau)

as the population version of
\widehat{\Delta}_{h,b}(t)
in (
16
) under Assumption
A2
. Here,
\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}_{T}
is the marginal probability distribution function of
T
.
Under the boundedness of
\mu(t,\bm{s})
by Assumption
A3
, we know that

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}-\mathbb{E}\left[m(T)\right]=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

Recall from (
14
) that
\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)=\int\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})d\widehat{P}(\bm{s}|t)
and
\theta(t)=\theta_{C}(t)=\int\beta_{2}(t,\bm{s})dP(\bm{s}|t)
with
\beta_{2}(t,\bm{s})=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu(t,\bm{s})
under Assumption
A2
.
Then, we have the following decomposition as:

\displaystyle\widehat{\Delta}_{h,b}(t)-\Delta(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\widetilde{t}=T_{i}}^{\widetilde{t}=t}\widehat{\theta}_{C}(\widetilde{t})d\widetilde{t}-\int\int_{\widetilde{t}=\tau}^{\widetilde{t}=t}\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})d\widetilde{t}d\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}_{T}(\tau)

(40)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\int\int_{\widetilde{t}=\tau}^{\widetilde{t}=t}\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})d\widetilde{t}\left[d\mathbb{P}_{n,T}(\tau)-d\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}_{T}(\tau)\right]}_{\textbf{Term (I)}}+\underbrace{\int\int_{\widetilde{t}=\tau}^{\widetilde{t}=t}\left[\widehat{\theta}_{C}(\widetilde{t})-\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})\right]d\widetilde{t}d\mathbb{P}_{n,T}(\tau)}_{\textbf{Term (II)}},

where
\mathbb{P}_{n,T}
is the empirical measure associated with
\{T_{1},...,T_{n}\}
.

\bullet

Term (I):
By Dvoretzky-Kiefer-Wolfowitz inequality
(
Dvoretzky et al., 1956
;
Massart, 1990
)
, we know that

\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\mathbb{P}_{n,T}(t)-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}_{T}(t)\right|=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

In addition, under Assumption
A3
and the compactness of the support
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
, we have that

\int\left[\int_{\widetilde{t}=\tau}^{\widetilde{t}=t}\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})d\widetilde{t}\right]^{2}d\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}_{T}(\tau)\leq\int|t-\tau|^{2}\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\left|\theta_{C}(t)\right|^{2}d\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}_{T}(\tau)<\infty

for any
t\in\mathcal{T}
. Thus, the (uniform) rate of convergence of Term (I) in (
40
) is

\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\int\int_{\widetilde{t}=\tau}^{\widetilde{t}=t}\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})d\widetilde{t}\left[d\mathbb{P}_{n,T}(\tau)-d\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}_{T}(\tau)\right]=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

\bullet

Term (II):
We first focus on establishing the (uniform) rate of convergence for
\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)-\theta_{C}(t)
. For any
t\in\mathcal{T}
, we note that

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)-\theta_{C}(t)

\displaystyle=\int\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})d\widehat{P}(\bm{s}|t)-\int\beta_{2}(t,\bm{s})dP(\bm{s}|t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})-\beta_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})\right]\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{i}}{\hslash}\right)}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{j}}{\hslash}\right)}+\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\beta_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})\cdot\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{i}}{\hslash}\right)}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{j}}{\hslash}\right)}-\int\beta_{2}(t,\bm{s})dP(\bm{s}|t)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{(t,\bm{S}_{i})\in\mathcal{E}\cap\left(\partial\mathcal{E}\ominus\hslash\right)}\frac{\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})-\beta_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})\right]\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{i}}{\hslash}\right)}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{j}}{\hslash}\right)}}_{\textbf{Term A}}+\underbrace{\sum_{(t,\bm{S}_{i})\in\partial\mathcal{E}\oplus\hslash}\frac{\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})-\beta_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})\right]\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{i}}{\hslash}\right)}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{j}}{\hslash}\right)}}_{\textbf{Term B}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\int\beta_{2}(t,\bm{s})\left[d\widehat{P}(\bm{s}|t)-dP(\bm{s}|t)\right]}_{\textbf{Term C}},

where
\partial\mathcal{E}\ominus\hslash=\left\{\bm{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{d+1}:\inf_{\bm{x}\in\partial\mathcal{E}}\left|\left|\bm{z}-\bm{x}\right|\right|_{2}\geq\hslash\right\}
and
\partial\mathcal{E}\oplus\hslash=\left\{\bm{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{d+1}:\inf_{\bm{x}\in\partial\mathcal{E}}\left|\left|\bm{z}-\bm{x}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\hslash\right\}
. In other words,
\mathcal{E}\cap\left(\partial\mathcal{E}\ominus\hslash\right)
indicates the interior region of
\mathcal{E}
that is at least
\hslash
away from its boundary
\partial\mathcal{E}
, while
\partial\mathcal{E}\oplus\hslash
is the region that is within a distance
\hslash
from the boundary
\partial\mathcal{E}
.

As for
Term A
, this part of the derivation is inspired by the proof of Theorem 1 in
Fan et al. (1998)
. We know from Assumption
A5
(c) that the proportion of
(t,\bm{S}_{i}),i=1,...,n
that lie in
\partial\mathcal{E}\oplus\hslash
is of order
O_{P}(\hslash)
. Thus, with probability tending to 1 as
n\to\infty
, there are at least
n(1-C\hslash)
points of
(t,\bm{S}_{i}),i=1,...,n
that lie in the interior region
\mathcal{E}\cap\left(\partial\mathcal{E}\ominus\hslash\right)
, where
C>0
is some absolute constant. We denote this collection of points by
\mathcal{I}
and let

W_{i}=\frac{|\mathcal{I}|\cdot\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{i}}{\hslash}\right)}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{\ell}}{\hslash}\right)}\quad\text{ for }\quad i\in\mathcal{I},

where
|\mathcal{I}|=n(1-C\hslash)
is the number of points in
\mathcal{I}
. Recall from (
36
) and the interleaving zero structures of
\bm{M}_{q}^{-1}
with an even number
q>0
that

\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})=\frac{1}{h\cdot p(t,\bm{s})}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{C_{K_{T}}\cdot Y_{j}}{hb^{d}}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{j}-\bm{s}}{b}\right)\left[1+A_{h,b}\right],

where
A_{h,b}=O(\max\{h,b\})+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log(hb^{d})|}{nhb^{d}}}\right)
uniformly over
t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
and
C_{K_{T}}>0
is a constant that only depends on the kernel
K_{T}
.
Now, we can rewrite
Term A
as:

Term A

(41)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(t,\bm{S}_{i})\in\mathcal{E}\cap\left(\partial\mathcal{E}\ominus\hslash\right)}\frac{\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})-\beta_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})\right]\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{i}}{\hslash}\right)}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{j}}{\hslash}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{I}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}W_{i}\left[\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})-\beta_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{I}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{W_{i}}{nh\cdot p(t,\bm{S}_{i})}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[r_{(t,\bm{S}_{i})}(T_{j},\bm{S}_{j})+\epsilon_{j}+A_{h,b}\right]}{hb^{d}}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{j}-\bm{S}_{i}}{b}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{nh^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{K_{T}}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)\left\{\frac{1}{|\mathcal{I}|b^{d}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{W_{i}\cdot K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{j}-\bm{S}_{i}}{b}\right)}{p(t,\bm{S}_{i})}\left[r_{(t,\bm{S}_{i})}(T_{j},\bm{S}_{j})+\epsilon_{j}+A_{h,b}\right]\right\},

where
r_{(t,\bm{S}_{i})}(T_{j},\bm{S}_{j})
is the remainder term of applying Taylor’s expansion to
\mu(T_{j},\bm{S}_{j})
around
(t,\bm{S}_{i})
as shown in (
29
).

We now study the (uniform) rate of convergence of the dominating term
\frac{1}{|\mathcal{I}|b^{d}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{W_{i}\cdot K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{j}-\bm{S}_{i}}{b}\right)}{p(t,\bm{S}_{i})}
inside the curly bracket. Notice that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{I}|b^{d}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{W_{i}\cdot K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{j}-\bm{S}_{i}}{b}\right)}{p(t,\bm{S}_{i})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{b^{d}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{i}}{\hslash}\right)\cdot K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{j}-\bm{S}_{i}}{b}\right)}{p(t,\bm{S}_{i})\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{\ell}}{\hslash}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{1}{n\hslash b^{d}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{i}}{\hslash}\right)}{p(t,\bm{S}_{i})}\cdot K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{j}-\bm{S}_{i}}{b}\right)}{\frac{1}{n\hslash}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{\ell}}{\hslash}\right)}.

The denominator
\frac{1}{n\hslash}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{\ell}}{\hslash}\right)
above is a simple kernel density estimator, and its (uniform) rate of convergence under Assumption
A6
(d) is

\sup_{\widetilde{t}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\frac{1}{n\hslash}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{\ell}}{\hslash}\right)-p_{T}(t)\right|=O(\hslash^{2})+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log\hslash|}{n\hslash}}\right)

when
\hslash\to 0
and
\frac{n\hslash}{|\log\hslash|},\frac{|\log\hslash|}{\log\log n}\to\infty
; see Theorem 1 in
Einmahl and Mason (2005)
.

For the numerator
\frac{1}{n\hslash b^{d}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{i}}{\hslash}\right)}{p(t,\bm{S}_{i})}\cdot K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{j}-\bm{S}_{i}}{b}\right)
, its conditional expectation can be computed as:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{n\hslash b^{d}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{i}}{\hslash}\right)}{p(t,\bm{S}_{i})}\cdot K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{j}-\bm{S}_{i}}{b}\right)\Big|\bm{S}_{j}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{K_{S}(0)}{nb^{d}\hslash\cdot p(t,\bm{S}_{j})}\int_{\mathcal{T}}\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t_{1}-t}{\hslash}\right)\cdot p(t_{1}|\bm{S}_{j})\,dt_{1}+\frac{|\mathcal{I}|}{nb^{d}\hslash}\int_{\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{S}}\frac{\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t_{1}-t}{\hslash}\right)\cdot K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{s}_{1}-\bm{S}_{j}}{b}\right)}{p(t,\bm{s}_{1})}\cdot p(t_{1},\bm{s}_{1})\,dt_{1}d\bm{s}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{K_{S}(0)}{nb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{S}_{j})}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bar{K}_{T}\left(u\right)\cdot p(t+u\hslash|\bm{S}_{j})\,du+\frac{|\mathcal{I}|}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\bar{K}_{T}\left(u\right)\cdot K_{S}\left(\bm{v}\right)}{p(t,\bm{S}_{j}+b\bm{v})}\cdot p(t+u\hslash,\bm{S}_{j}+b\bm{v})\,dud\bm{v}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{=}}\frac{K_{S}(0)\left[p(t|\bm{S}_{j})+\frac{\hslash^{2}\bar{\kappa}_{2}^{(T)}}{2}\cdot\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}p(t|\bm{S}_{j})+o(\hslash^{2})\right]}{nb^{d}\cdot p(t,\bm{S}_{j})}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{|\mathcal{I}|}{n}\left[1+\frac{\bar{\kappa}_{2}^{(T)}\hslash^{2}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}p(t,\bm{S}_{j}+b\bm{v})}{p(t,\bm{S}_{j}+b\bm{v})}\cdot K_{S}(\bm{v})\,d\bm{v}+o(\hslash^{2})\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{=}}\frac{K_{S}(0)}{nb^{d}}+\frac{|\mathcal{I}|}{n}+O_{P}\left(\hslash^{2}+\frac{\hslash^{2}}{nb^{d}}\right)

\displaystyle=1+O_{P}\left(\hslash\right)

when
\hslash,h,b\to 0
and
\frac{nhb^{d}}{|\log(\hslash hb^{d})|}\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
, where
\bar{\kappa}_{2}^{(T)}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}u^{2}\bar{K}_{T}(u)\,du
as well as we apply Taylor’s expansions on
p(\cdot|\bm{S}_{j})
and
p
and use the boundedness of their derivatives under Assumption
A4
in (i). Additionally, its conditional variance can be upper bounded by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left[\frac{1}{n\hslash b^{d}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{i}}{\hslash}\right)}{p(t,\bm{S}_{i})}\cdot K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{j}-\bm{S}_{i}}{b}\right)\Big|\bm{S}_{j}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2}b^{2d}\hslash^{2}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\bar{K}_{T}^{2}\left(\frac{t-T_{i}}{\hslash}\right)}{p^{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})}\cdot K_{S}^{2}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{j}-\bm{S}_{i}}{b}\right)\Big|\bm{S}_{j}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{K_{S}^{2}(0)}{n^{2}b^{2d}\hslash^{2}}\int_{\mathcal{T}}\frac{\bar{K}_{T}^{2}\left(\frac{t_{1}-t}{\hslash}\right)}{p^{2}(t,\bm{S}_{j})}\cdot p(t_{1}|\bm{S}_{j})\,dt_{1}+\frac{|\mathcal{I}|}{n^{2}b^{2d}\hslash^{2}}\int_{\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{S}}\frac{\bar{K}_{T}^{2}\left(\frac{t_{1}-t}{\hslash}\right)\cdot K_{S}^{2}\left(\frac{\bm{s}_{1}-\bm{S}_{j}}{b}\right)}{p^{2}(t,\bm{s}_{1})}\cdot p(t_{1},\bm{s}_{1})\,dt_{1}d\bm{s}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{K_{S}^{2}(0)}{n^{2}b^{2d}\hslash}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\bar{K}_{T}^{2}\left(u\right)}{p^{2}(t,\bm{S}_{j})}\cdot p(t+u\hslash|\bm{S}_{j})\,du+\frac{|\mathcal{I}|}{n^{2}b^{d}\hslash}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\bar{K}_{T}^{2}\left(u\right)\cdot K_{S}^{2}\left(\bm{v}\right)}{p^{2}(t,\bm{S}_{j}+b\bm{v})}\cdot p(t+u\hslash,\bm{S}_{j}+b\bm{v})\,dud\bm{v}

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{nb^{d}\hslash}\right)

when
\hslash,h,b\to 0
and
\frac{n\max\{h,\hslash\}b^{d}}{|\log(\hslash hb^{d})|}\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
, where the last equality again uses the boundedness of
p(t|\bm{S}_{j})
and
p
under Assumption
A4
and the fact that
(t,\bm{S}_{i})\in\mathcal{E}\cap\left(\partial\mathcal{E}\ominus\hslash\right)
for
i\in\mathcal{I}
. Thus,

\frac{1}{n\hslash b^{d}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{i}}{\hslash}\right)}{p(t,\bm{S}_{i})}\cdot K_{S}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{j}-\bm{S}_{i}}{b}\right)=1+O_{P}(\hslash)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nb^{d}\hslash}}\right).

Plugging the above results back into (
41
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\textbf{Term A}&=\frac{1}{nh^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{K_{T}}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)\\
&\quad\times\left\{\left[\frac{1}{P_{T}(t)}+O_{P}(\hslash)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log\hslash|}{n\hslash}}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{nb^{d}\hslash}}\right)\right]\left[\epsilon_{j}+A_{h,b}+\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}r_{(t,\bm{S}_{i})}(T_{j},\bm{S}_{j})\right]\right\}\end{split}

(42)

Now, we know from Lemma
3
that

\sup_{(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{E}}\left|\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})-\beta_{2}(t,\bm{s})\right|=O\left(h^{q}+b^{2}+\frac{\max\{b,h\}^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log(hb^{d})|}{nh^{3}b^{d}}}\right)

when
q>0
is even. Since
Term A
is a weighted sum of
\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})-\beta_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})
, the bias of
Term A
, which comes from the term involving the residual sum
\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}r_{(t,\bm{S}_{i})}(T_{j},S_{j})
, will not be larger than the (uniform) bias of
\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})-\beta_{2}(t,\bm{s})
,
i.e.
,

\left|\mathrm{Bias}\left[\textbf{Term A}\right]\right|=O\left(h^{q}+b^{2}+\frac{\max\{b,h\}^{4}}{h}\right).

Thus, it remains to derive the rate of convergence for the stochastic variation of (
42
). Note that when
\hslash,h,b\to 0
and
\frac{n\max\{h,\hslash\}b^{d}}{|\log(\hslash hb^{d})|},\frac{|\log(h\hslash b^{d})|}{\log\log n}\to\infty
, the variance of
Term A
is dominated by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left[\textbf{Term A}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathrm{Var}\left[\frac{1}{nh^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{C_{K_{T}}}{p_{T}(t)}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{K_{T}}^{2}}{nh^{4}\cdot p_{T}^{2}(t)}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)^{2}K_{T}^{2}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{K_{T}}^{2}}{nh^{4}\cdot p_{T}^{2}(t)}\int_{\mathcal{T}}\left(\frac{t_{1}-t}{h}\right)^{2}K_{T}^{2}\left(\frac{t_{1}-t}{h}\right)\cdot p_{T}(t_{1})\,dt_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{K_{T}}^{2}}{nh^{3}\cdot p_{T}^{2}(t)}\int_{\mathbb{R}}u^{2}K_{T}^{2}\left(u\right)\cdot p_{T}(t+uh)\,du

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{1}{nh^{3}}\right)

for any
t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
. Furthermore, under our Assumption
A6
(b), the result in Theorem 4 of
Einmahl and Mason (2005)
can be applied to derive that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\frac{1}{nh^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{C_{K_{T}}}{p_{T}(t)}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{nh^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{C_{K_{T}}}{p_{T}(t)}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{3}}}\right)

when
\frac{nh^{3}}{|\log h|},\frac{|\log h|}{\log\log n}\to\infty
; see also
Giné and Guillou (2002)
;
Chacón et al. (2011)
. Therefore, combining with the bias rate of
Term A
, we obtain that

\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\textbf{Term A}\right|=O\left(h^{q}+b^{2}+\frac{\max\{b,h\}^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{3}}}\right)

when
\hslash,h,b,\frac{\max\{h,b\}^{4}}{h}\to 0
and
\frac{n\max\{h,\hslash\}b^{d}}{|\log(\hslash hb^{d})|},\frac{|\log(h\hslash b^{d})|}{\log\log n},\frac{nh^{3}}{|\log h|}\to\infty
.

As for
Term B
, we leverage the compact support of
\bar{K}_{T}
(Assumption
A6
(c)) to argue that
|t-T_{i}|=O(\hslash)
for any
(t,\bm{S}_{i})\in\partial\mathcal{E}\oplus\hslash
. Then, under Assumptions
A3
and
A6
(a), we know that both
\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{s})
and
\beta_{2}(t,\bm{s})
are Lipschitz so that

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})-\beta_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})-\widehat{\beta}_{2}(T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})\right|+\left|\widehat{\beta}_{2}(T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})-\beta_{2}(T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})\right|+\left|\beta_{2}(T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})-\beta_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})\right|

\displaystyle\leq O_{P}\left(\hslash\right)+\left|\widehat{\beta}_{2}(T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})-\beta_{2}(T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})\right|.

In addition, by Assumption
A5
(c), we know that the proportion of
(t,\bm{S}_{i}),i=1,...,n
that lie in
\partial\mathcal{E}\oplus\hslash
is of order
O_{P}(\hslash)
. Thus,
\sum\limits_{(t,\bm{S}_{i})\in\partial\mathcal{E}\oplus\hslash}\frac{\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{i}}{\hslash}\right)}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{j}}{\hslash}\right)}=O_{P}(\hslash)
and hence,

Term B

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(t,\bm{S}_{i})\in\partial\mathcal{E}\oplus\hslash}\frac{\left|\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})-\beta_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})\right|\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{i}}{\hslash}\right)}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{j}}{\hslash}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(t,\bm{S}_{i})\in\partial\mathcal{E}\oplus\hslash}\frac{\left[\left|\widehat{\beta}_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})-\widehat{\beta}_{2}(T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})\right|+\left|\beta_{2}(T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})-\beta_{2}(t,\bm{S}_{i})\right|\right]\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{i}}{\hslash}\right)}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{j}}{\hslash}\right)}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{(t,\bm{S}_{i})\in\partial\mathcal{E}\oplus\hslash}\frac{\left|\widehat{\beta}_{2}(T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})-\beta_{2}(T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})\right|\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{i}}{\hslash}\right)}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{j}}{\hslash}\right)}

\displaystyle=O_{P}(\hslash^{2})+\sum_{(t,\bm{S}_{i})\in\partial\mathcal{E}\oplus\hslash}\frac{\left|\widehat{\beta}_{2}(T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})-\beta_{2}(T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})\right|\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{i}}{\hslash}\right)}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{j}}{\hslash}\right)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{=}}O\left(h^{q}+b^{2}+\frac{\max\{b,h\}^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\hslash^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{3}}}\right)

when
q>0
is even,
\hslash,h,b,\frac{\max\{h,b\}^{4}}{h}\to 0
, and
\frac{n\max\{h,\hslash\}b^{d}}{|\log(\hslash hb^{d})|},\frac{|\log(h\hslash b^{d})|}{\log\log n},\frac{nh^{3}}{|\log h|}\to\infty
. Here, in (iii), we can use the same arguments for
Term A
to argue that the (uniform) rate of convergence of
\sum_{(t,\bm{S}_{i})\in\partial\mathcal{E}\oplus\hslash}\frac{\left|\widehat{\beta}_{2}(T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})-\beta_{2}(T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})\right|\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{i}}{\hslash}\right)}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bar{K}_{T}\left(\frac{t-T_{j}}{\hslash}\right)}
is at most
O\left(h^{q}+b^{2}+\frac{\max\{b,h\}^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{3}}}\right)
, because
\widehat{\beta}_{2}
and
\beta_{2}
are evaluated on the sample points
(T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})\in\mathcal{E}
.

As for
Term C
, under Assumptions
A3
and
A6
(d), we utilize Theorem 3 in
Einmahl and Mason (2005)
to derive its uniform rate of convergence as:

\textbf{Term C}\leq\sup_{(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{S}}|\beta_{2}(t,\bm{s})|\cdot\sup_{(t,\bm{s})\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{S}}\left|\widehat{P}(\bm{s}|t)-P(\bm{s}|t)\right|\cdot|\mathcal{S}|=O(\hslash^{2})+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log\hslash|}{n\hslash}}\right),

where the Lebesgue measure
|\mathcal{S}|
is finite due to the compactness of
\mathcal{S}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)-\theta_{C}(t)\right|=O\left(h^{q}+b^{2}+\frac{\max\{b,h\}^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log(h)|}{nh^{3}}}+\hslash^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{|\log\hslash|}{n\hslash}}\right)

when
q>0
is even,
h,b,\hslash,\frac{\max\{b,h\}^{4}}{h}\to 0
, and
\frac{n\max\{h,\hslash\}b^{d}}{|\log(\hslash hb^{d})|},\frac{|\log(h\hslash b^{d})|}{\log\log n},\frac{nh^{3}}{|\log h|}\to\infty
.

Finally, plugging the results back into (
40
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{\Delta}_{h,b,\hslash}(t)-\Delta(t)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\int\int_{\widetilde{t}=\tau}^{\widetilde{t}=t}\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})d\widetilde{t}\left[d\mathbb{P}_{n,T}(\tau)-d\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}_{T}(\tau)\right]\right|+\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{T_{i}}^{t}\left[\widehat{\theta}_{C}(\widetilde{t})-\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})\right]d\widetilde{t}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\int\int_{\widetilde{t}=\tau}^{\widetilde{t}=t}\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})d\widetilde{t}\left[d\mathbb{P}_{n,T}(\tau)-d\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}_{T}(\tau)\right]\right|+\sup_{t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}}|t_{1}-t_{2}|\cdot\sup_{\widetilde{t}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{\theta}_{C}(\widetilde{t})-\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})\right|

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)+O\left(h^{q}+b^{2}+\frac{\max\{b,h\}^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{3}}}+\hslash^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{|\log\hslash|}{n\hslash}}\right)

when
q>0
is even,
h,b,\hslash,\frac{\max\{b,h\}^{4}}{h}\to 0
, and
\frac{n\max\{h,\hslash\}b^{d}}{|\log(\hslash hb^{d})|},\frac{|\log(h\hslash b^{d})|}{\log\log n},\frac{nh^{3}}{|\log h|}\to\infty
, where
\sup_{t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}}|t_{1}-t_{2}|<\infty
due to the compactness of
\mathcal{T}=\mathrm{proj}_{T}(\mathcal{E})
. The proof is thus completed.
∎

C.4
Proof of Lemma
5

Lemma 5

(Asymptotic linearity)
.

Let
q\geq 2
in the local polynomial regression for estimating
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu(t,\bm{s})
and
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{T}
be a compact set so that
p_{T}(t)
is uniformly bounded away from 0 within
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
. Suppose that Assumptions
A1
,
A2
,
A3
,
A4
,
A5
, and
A6
hold. Then, if
b\lesssim h\asymp n^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}
and
\hslash\asymp n^{-\frac{1}{\varpi}}
for some
\gamma,\varpi>0
such that
\frac{nh^{5}}{\log n}\to c_{1}
and
\frac{n\hslash^{5}}{\log n}\to c_{2}
for some finite number
c_{1},c_{2}\geq 0
and
\sqrt{nh^{3}\max\{h,\hslash\}^{4}},\frac{h^{3}\log n}{\hslash},\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n\hslash}},\frac{\log n}{n\max\{h,\hslash\}b^{d}}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, then for any
t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
, we have that

\sqrt{nh^{3}}\left[\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)-\theta(t)\right]=\mathbb{G}_{n}\bar{\varphi}_{t}+o_{P}(1)\quad\text{ and }\quad\sqrt{nh^{3}}\left[\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)\right]=\mathbb{G}_{n}\varphi_{t}+o_{P}(1),

where
\bar{\varphi}_{t}(Y,T,\bm{S})=\frac{C_{K_{T}}[Y-\mu(T,\bm{S})]}{\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(t)}\left(\frac{T-t}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T-t}{h}\right)
for some constant
C_{K_{T}}>0
that only depends on the kernel
K_{T}
and

\displaystyle\varphi_{t}\left(Y,T,\bm{S}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left[\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\bar{\varphi}_{\widetilde{t}}(Y,T,\bm{S})\,d\widetilde{t}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left\{\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[Y-\mu(T,\bm{S})\right]}{\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)d\widetilde{t}\right\}.

Furthermore, we have the following uniform results as:

\displaystyle\left|\sqrt{nh^{3}}\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)-\theta(t)\right|-\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}\bar{\varphi}_{t}|\right|\asymp\left|\sqrt{nh^{3}}\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)\right|-\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}\varphi_{t}|\right|

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}\max\{h,\hslash\}^{4}}+\sqrt{\frac{h^{3}\log n}{\hslash}}+\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n\hslash}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{nb^{d}\hslash}}\right).

Proof of Lemma
5
.

The entire proof consists of three steps:
Step 1
establishes the asymptotic linearity of
\sqrt{nh^{3}}\left[\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)-\theta_{C}(t)\right]
for any
t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
.
Step 2
derives the exact rate of convergence for the coupling between
\sqrt{nh^{3}}\sup\limits_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)-\theta_{C}(t)\right|
and
\sup\limits_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\bar{\varphi}_{t}\right|
.
Step 3
extends the arguments in the previous two steps using the form of a V-statistics
(
Shieh, 2014
)
to prove the asymptotic linearity of
\sqrt{nh^{3}}\left[\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)\right]
. Note that
\theta(t)=\theta_{C}(t)
under Assumption
A2
.

\bullet

Step 1:
Recalling from (
42
) and our calculations for
Term B
and
Term C
in the proof of
Theorem 4
, we know that

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)-\theta_{C}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{nh^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{K_{T}}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\left\{\left[\frac{1}{P_{T}(t)}+O_{P}(\hslash)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log\hslash|}{n\hslash}}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{nb^{d}\hslash}}\right)\right]\left[\epsilon_{j}+A_{h,b}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(h^{q}+b^{2}+\frac{\max\{b,h\}^{4}}{h}\right)+O_{P}\left(\hslash^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{|\log\hslash|}{n\hslash}}\right),

where
A_{h,b}=O(\max\{h,b\})+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log(hb^{d})|}{nhb^{d}}}\right)
uniformly over
t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
and
C_{K_{T}}>0
is a constant that only depends on the kernel
K_{T}
. Then, when
q\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{nh^{3}}\left[\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)-\theta_{C}(t)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{nh}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{C_{K_{T}}\cdot\epsilon_{j}}{p_{T}(t)}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{nh^{7}}+\sqrt{|\log h|}\right)\cdot O_{P}\left(\hslash+\sqrt{\frac{|\log\hslash|}{n\hslash}}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{nb^{d}\hslash}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}\max\{h^{2q},b^{4}\}}+\sqrt{nh\max\{h,b\}^{8}}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}\hslash^{4}}+\sqrt{\frac{h^{3}|\log\hslash|}{\hslash}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{nh}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{C_{K_{T}}[Y_{j}-\mu(T_{j},\bm{S}_{j})]}{p_{T}(t)}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{j}-t}{h}\right)+o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{n}\bar{\varphi}_{t}+o_{P}(1)

when
n,b,\hslash,nh^{3}\max\{h^{2q},b^{4},\hslash^{4}\},nh\max\{h,b\}^{8},nh^{7}\hslash,\frac{h^{3}|\log\hslash|}{\hslash}\to 0
and
\frac{n\hslash}{|\log\hslash|\cdot|\log h|},\frac{n\max\{h,\hslash\}b^{d}}{|\log(\hslash hb^{d})|}\to\infty
, where

\bar{\varphi}_{t}(Y,T,\bm{S})=\frac{C_{K_{T}}[Y-\mu(T,\bm{S})]}{\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(t)}\left(\frac{T-t}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T-t}{h}\right)
and
\mathbb{G}_{n}=\sqrt{n}\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\right)
.

\bullet

Step 2:
From our results in
Step 1
, we know that

\displaystyle\sqrt{nh^{3}}\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)-\theta_{C}(t)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\bar{\varphi}_{t}\right|+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{nh^{7}}+\sqrt{|\log h|}\right)\cdot O_{P}\left(\hslash+\sqrt{\frac{|\log\hslash|}{n\hslash}}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{nb^{d}\hslash}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}\max\{h^{2q},b^{4}\}}+\sqrt{nh\max\{h,b\}^{8}}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}\hslash^{4}}+\sqrt{\frac{h^{3}|\log\hslash|}{\hslash}}\right).

When
q\geq 2
,
b\lesssim h\asymp n^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}
, and
\hslash\asymp n^{-\frac{1}{\varpi}}
for some
\gamma,\varpi>0
such that
\frac{nh^{5}}{\log n}\to c_{1}
and
\frac{n\hslash^{5}}{\log n}\to c_{2}
for some finite number
c_{1},c_{2}\geq 0
, we can further simplify the above rate of convergence as:

\displaystyle\left|\sqrt{nh^{3}}\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)-\theta_{C}(t)\right|-\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\bar{\varphi}_{t}\right|\right|=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}\max\{h,\hslash\}^{4}}+\sqrt{\frac{h^{3}\log n}{\hslash}}+\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n\hslash}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{nb^{d}\hslash}}\right),

provided that
\sqrt{nh^{3}\max\{h,\hslash\}^{4}},\frac{h^{3}\log n}{\hslash},\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n\hslash}},\frac{\log n}{n\max\{h,\hslash\}b^{d}}\to 0
.

\bullet

Step 3:
Recalling from (
10
) for the definition of the integral estimator
\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)
and (
40
), we know that

\displaystyle\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[Y_{i}+\int_{\widetilde{t}=T_{i}}^{\widetilde{t}=t}\widehat{\theta}_{C}(\widetilde{t})d\widetilde{t}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[m(T)\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{T}^{t}\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})\,d\widetilde{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}-\mathbb{E}(Y)}_{\textbf{Term I}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{T_{i}}^{t}\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})\,d\widetilde{t}-\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{T}^{t}\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})\,d\widetilde{t}\right]}_{\textbf{Term II}}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{T_{i}}^{t}\left[\widehat{\theta}_{C}(\widetilde{t})-\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})\right]d\widetilde{t}

where we use Assumption
A2
to obtain the second equality above.

As for
Term I
and
Term II
, under Assumption
A3
and the condition
\mathbb{E}\left(\epsilon^{4}\right)<\infty
, it is valid that
\mathrm{Var}(Y)<\infty
and
\mathrm{Var}\left[\int_{T}^{t}\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})\,d\widetilde{t}\right]<\infty
. Thus, by Chebyshev’s inequality, we know that

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}-\mathbb{E}(Y)=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\quad\text{ and }\quad\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{T_{i}}^{t}\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})\,d\widetilde{t}-\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{T}^{t}\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})\,d\widetilde{t}\right]=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

Furthermore, under Assumption
A3
,
\left|\int_{T}^{t_{1}}\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})\,d\widetilde{t}-\int_{T}^{t_{2}}\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})\,d\widetilde{t}\right|\leq\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}|\theta_{C}(t)|\cdot|t_{1}-t_{2}|
. Together with the compactness of
\mathcal{T}
and Example 19.7 in
van der Vaart (1998)
, we also deduce that

\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{T_{i}}^{t}\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})\,d\widetilde{t}-\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{T}^{t}\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})\,d\widetilde{t}\right]\right|=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

Therefore, together with our results in
Step 1
, we have that

\displaystyle\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{T_{i}}^{t}\left[\widehat{\theta}_{C}(\widetilde{t})-\theta_{C}(\widetilde{t})\right]d\widetilde{t}+O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}h^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\int_{T_{i_{1}}}^{t}\frac{C_{K_{T}}\cdot\epsilon_{i_{2}}}{p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T_{i_{2}}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{i_{2}}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)d\widetilde{t}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{T_{i}}^{t}\bar{A}_{h,\hslash,b}\,d\widetilde{t}+O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right),

where
\bar{A}_{h,\hslash,b}=O_{P}\left(\max\{h^{2},\hslash^{2}\},\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n\hslash}}+\frac{\log n}{n\sqrt{\hslash h^{3}}}+\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{n\sqrt{b^{d}\hslash h^{3}}}\right)
uniformly over
t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
under our conditions on the bandwidth parameters
h,\hslash,b>0
. Since
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{T_{i}}^{t}\bar{A}_{h,\hslash,b}\,d\widetilde{t}\leq|\bar{A}_{h,\hslash,b}|\cdot\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|t-T_{i}|\right)=|\bar{A}_{h,\hslash,b}|\cdot O_{P}(1)
, we know that

\displaystyle\sqrt{nh^{3}}\left[\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)\right]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{n^{\frac{3}{2}}}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\int_{T_{i_{1}}}^{t}\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[Y_{i_{2}}-\mu(T_{i_{2}},\bm{S}_{i_{2}})\right]}{\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T_{i_{2}}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{i_{2}}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)d\widetilde{t}}_{\textbf{Term III}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{O_{P}\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}\max\{h,\hslash\}^{4}}+\sqrt{\frac{h^{3}\log n}{\hslash}}+\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n\hslash}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{nb^{d}\hslash}}\right)}_{\textbf{Term IV}},

where
Term IV
is of order
o_{P}\left(1\right)
by our results in
Step 2
. Now, to derive the asymptotic linearity of
\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)
, it remains to tackle
Term III
above, which takes a form of V-statistics with a symmetric “kernel” defined as:

\displaystyle\Lambda_{t}(\bm{U}_{i_{1}},\bm{U}_{i_{2}})

\displaystyle=\int_{T_{i_{1}}}^{t}\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[Y_{i_{2}}-\mu(T_{i_{2}},\bm{S}_{i_{2}})\right]}{2\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T_{i_{2}}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{i_{2}}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)d\widetilde{t}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{T_{i_{2}}}^{t}\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[Y_{i_{1}}-\mu(T_{i_{1}},\bm{S}_{i_{1}})\right]}{2\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T_{i_{1}}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{i_{1}}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)d\widetilde{t}

with
\bm{U}_{i}=\left(Y_{i},T_{i},\bm{S}_{i}\right)
for
i=1,...,n
. By Pascal’s rule, we know that

Term III

\displaystyle=2\sqrt{n}\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\right)\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\lambda_{t}+\sqrt{n}\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\right)^{2}\Lambda_{t}+\sqrt{n}\cdot\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}^{2}\Lambda_{t}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left\{\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left[\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[Y-\mu(T,\bm{S})\right]}{\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)\right]d\widetilde{t}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int_{T}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{U}_{1}}\left[\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[Y_{1}-\mu(T_{1},\bm{S}_{1})\right]}{\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T_{1}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{1}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)\right]d\widetilde{t}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{n}\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\right)^{2}\left\{\int_{T_{i_{1}}}^{t}\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[Y_{i_{2}}-\mu(T_{i_{2}},\bm{S}_{i_{2}})\right]}{2\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T_{i_{2}}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{i_{2}}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)d\widetilde{t}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{n}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left\{\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{U}_{2}}\left[\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[Y_{2}-\mu(T_{2},\bm{S}_{2})\right]}{2\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T_{2}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{2}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)\right]d\widetilde{t}\right\}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left[\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[Y-\mu(T,\bm{S})\right]}{\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)d\widetilde{t}\right]\right\}}_{\textbf{Term IIIa}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\sqrt{n}\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\right)^{2}\left\{\int_{T_{i_{1}}}^{t}\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[Y_{i_{2}}-\mu(T_{i_{2}},\bm{S}_{i_{2}})\right]}{2\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T_{i_{2}}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{i_{2}}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)d\widetilde{t}\right\}}_{\textbf{Term IIIb}},

because
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{U}_{2}}\left[\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[Y_{2}-\mu(T_{2},\bm{S}_{2})\right]}{2\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T_{2}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{2}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)\right]=0
for any
\widetilde{t}\in\mathcal{T}
. By Lemma
C.1
, we know that
\mathrm{Var}\left[\textbf{Term IIIa}\right]
is a finite positive number, so
Term IIIa
is the asymptotically dominating term.

As for
Term IIIb
, we recall from Assumption
A6
(b) by holding
\bm{z}
that

\mathcal{K}=\left\{t^{\prime}\mapsto\left(\frac{t^{\prime}-t}{h}\right)^{k_{1}}K\left(\frac{t^{\prime}-t}{h}\right):t\in\mathcal{T},h>0,k_{1}=0,1\right\}

is a bounded VC-type class of measurable functions on
\mathbb{R}
. Under Assumption
A3
and the condition that
\mathbb{E}(\epsilon^{4})<\infty
, we deduce by Theorem 4 in
Einmahl and Mason (2005)
that with probability 1,

\displaystyle\sup_{\widetilde{t}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left[\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[Y-\mu(T,\bm{S})\right]}{\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)\right]\right|=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\log n}\right)

when
h\asymp n^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}
. Thus, by Chebyshev’s inequality,

Term IIIb

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\right)\left\{\int_{T_{i_{1}}}^{t}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left[\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[Y_{i_{2}}-\mu(T_{i_{2}},\bm{S}_{i_{2}})\right]}{2\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T_{i_{2}}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{i_{2}}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)\right]d\widetilde{t}\right\}

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left\{\left[\int_{T_{i_{1}}}^{t}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left[\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[Y_{i_{2}}-\mu(T_{i_{2}},\bm{S}_{i_{2}})\right]}{2\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T_{i_{2}}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{i_{2}}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)\right]d\widetilde{t}\right]^{2}\right\}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{=}}O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)=o_{P}(1).

Here, the equality (i) follows from the calculation that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\left[\int_{T_{i_{1}}}^{t}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left[\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[Y_{i_{2}}-\mu(T_{i_{2}},\bm{S}_{i_{2}})\right]}{2\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T_{i_{2}}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T_{i_{2}}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)\right]d\widetilde{t}\right]^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{E}\left[|t-T_{i_{1}}|^{2}\cdot\sup_{\widetilde{t}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left[\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[Y-\mu(T,\bm{S})\right]}{\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)\right]\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=O\left(\log n\right),

where (ii) applies the mean-value theorem for integrals.

As a summary, we conclude that

\displaystyle\sqrt{nh^{3}}\left[\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left[\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[Y-\mu(T,\bm{S})\right]}{\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)d\widetilde{t}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}\max\{h,\hslash\}^{4}}+\sqrt{\frac{h^{3}\log n}{\hslash}}+\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n\hslash}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{nb^{d}\hslash}}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{n}\varphi_{t}+o_{P}(1)

when
q\geq 2
,
b\lesssim h\asymp n^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}
, and
\hslash\asymp n^{-\frac{1}{\varpi}}
for some
\gamma,\varpi>0
such that
\frac{nh^{5}}{\log n}\to c_{1}
and
\frac{n\hslash^{5}}{\log n}\to c_{2}
for some finite number
c_{1},c_{2}\geq 0
as well as
\sqrt{nh^{3}\max\{h,\hslash\}^{4}},\frac{h^{3}\log n}{\hslash},\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n\hslash}},\frac{\log n}{n\max\{h,\hslash\}b^{d}}\to 0
, where

\displaystyle\varphi_{t}\left(Y,T,\bm{S}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left[\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\bar{\varphi}_{\widetilde{t}}(Y,T,\bm{S})\,d\widetilde{t}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left\{\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[Y-\mu(T,\bm{S})\right]}{\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)d\widetilde{t}\right\}.

We also know from our above results that

\displaystyle\left|\sqrt{nh^{3}}\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)\right|-\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\varphi_{t}\right|\right|=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}\max\{h,\hslash\}^{4}}+\sqrt{\frac{h^{3}\log n}{\hslash}}+\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n\hslash}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{nb^{d}\hslash}}\right)

under our conditions on the bandwidth parameters. The proof is thus completed.
∎

Lemma C.1
.

Let
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{T}
be a compact set so that
p_{T}(t)
is uniformly bounded away from 0 within
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
. Suppose that Assumptions
A3
,
A4
,
A5
, and
A6
hold. Then, when
h,b,\hslash
are sufficiently small as
n\to\infty
, there exist constants
\underline{\sigma},\bar{\sigma}>0
such that
\underline{\sigma}^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\varphi_{t}(Y,T,\bm{S})^{2}\right]\leq\bar{\sigma}^{2}
for any
t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
, where
\varphi_{t}
is defined in (
17
). Here, all the constants
\underline{\sigma},\bar{\sigma}>0
are independent of
h,b,\hslash
and
n
. Furthermore, the same upper and lower bounds apply to
\mathrm{Var}\left[\varphi_{t}(Y,T,\bm{S})\right]
. Finally, the same result holds true when we replace
\varphi_{t}
with
\bar{\varphi}_{t}
for any
t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
.

Proof of Lemma
C.1
.

Recall from (
17
) that for any
t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\varphi_{t}(Y,T,\bm{S})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\{\left[\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left\{\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\frac{C_{K_{T}}\left[Y-\mu(T,\bm{S})\right]}{\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)d\widetilde{t}\right\}\right]^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{K_{T}}^{2}\sigma^{2}}{h}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left\{\left[\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left\{\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\frac{1}{p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{T-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)d\widetilde{t}\right\}\right]^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{K_{T}}^{2}\sigma^{2}}{h}\int_{\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{S}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left[\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\frac{1}{p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{t_{2}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{t_{2}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)d\widetilde{t}\right]\right\}^{2}p(t_{2},\bm{s}_{2})\,d\bm{s}_{2}dt_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{K_{T}}^{2}\sigma^{2}}{h}\int_{\mathcal{T}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left[\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\frac{1}{p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{t_{2}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{t_{2}-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)\sqrt{p_{T}(t_{2})}\,d\widetilde{t}\right]\right\}^{2}dt_{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{=}}C_{K_{T}}^{2}\sigma^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left[\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\frac{u}{p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\cdot K_{T}\left(u\right)\sqrt{p_{T}(\widetilde{t}+uh)}\,d\widetilde{t}\right]\right\}^{2}du

\displaystyle=C_{K_{T}}^{2}\sigma^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}u^{2}\cdot K_{T}^{2}(u)\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left[\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\frac{\sqrt{p_{T}(\widetilde{t}+uh)}}{p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\,d\widetilde{t}\right]\right\}^{2}du

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{=}}C_{K_{T}}^{2}\sigma^{2}\nu_{2}^{(T)}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left[\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}}\,d\widetilde{t}\right]\right\}^{2}+O(h)

where (i) applies a change of variable
u=\frac{t_{2}-\widetilde{t}}{h}
and (ii) utilizes a Taylor’s expansion on
p_{T}(\widetilde{t}+uh)
under Assumption
A4
. Now, since
p_{T}
is upper bounded and lower bounded away from 0 within
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
, then

\mathbb{E}\left[\varphi_{t}(Y,T,\bm{S})^{2}\right]\geq C_{K_{T}}^{2}\sigma^{2}\nu_{2}^{(T)}\left[\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}(t-T_{1})\cdot\sup\limits_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\sqrt{p_{T}(t)}}\right]^{2}+O(h)\geq\underline{\sigma}^{2}

and

\mathbb{E}\left[\varphi_{t}(Y,T,\bm{S})^{2}\right]\leq C_{K_{T}}^{2}\sigma^{2}\nu_{2}^{(T)}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left[(t-T_{1})\right]^{2}\cdot\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{p_{T}(t)}}\right\}+O(h)\leq\bar{\sigma}^{2}

for some constants
\underline{\sigma},\bar{\sigma}>0
that are independent of
h,b,\hslash
and
n
when
n
is sufficiently large.

As for the influence function
\bar{\varphi}_{t}
of
\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)
in (
17
), we use the similar arguments to derive that for any
t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bar{\varphi}_{t}(Y,T,\bm{S})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\{\left[\frac{C_{K_{T}}[Y-\mu(T,\bm{S})]}{\sqrt{h}\cdot p_{T}(t)}\left(\frac{T-t}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{T-t}{h}\right)\right]^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{K_{T}}^{2}\sigma^{2}}{h}\int_{\mathcal{T}}\frac{1}{p_{T}^{2}(t)}\left(\frac{t_{1}-t}{h}\right)^{2}K_{T}^{2}\left(\frac{t_{1}-t}{h}\right)p_{T}(t_{2})\,dt_{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{=}}C_{K_{T}}^{2}\sigma^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{u^{2}}{p_{T}^{2}(t)}\cdot K_{T}^{2}(u)\cdot p_{T}(t+uh)\,du

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iv)}}}{{=}}\frac{C_{K_{T}}^{2}\sigma^{2}\nu_{2}^{(T)}}{p_{T}(t)}+O(h),

where (iii) applies a change of variable
u=\frac{t_{1}-t}{h}
and (ii) utilizes a Taylor’s expansion on
p_{T}(t+uh)
under Assumption
A4
. Since
p_{T}(t)
is upper bounded and lower bounded away from 0 for any
t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
, we have that

\underline{\sigma}^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\bar{\varphi}_{t}(Y,T,\bm{S})^{2}\right]=\frac{C_{K_{T}}^{2}\sigma^{2}\nu_{2}^{(T)}}{p_{T}(t)}+O(h)\leq\bar{\sigma}^{2}

when
h
are sufficiently small as
n\to\infty
, where
\underline{\sigma},\bar{\sigma}>0
can be again chosen to be independent of
h,b,\hslash
and
n
. The results follow.
∎

C.5
Proof of
Theorem 6

Before proving
Theorem 6
, we first study some auxiliary results. Recall that the class
\mathcal{G}
of measurable functions on
\mathbb{R}^{d+1}
is VC-type if there exist constants
A_{2},\upsilon_{2}>0
such that for any
0<\epsilon<1
,

\sup_{Q}N\left(\mathcal{G},L_{2}(Q),\epsilon\left|\left|G\right|\right|_{L_{2}(Q)}\right)\leq\left(\frac{A_{2}}{\epsilon}\right)^{\upsilon_{2}},

where
N\left(\mathcal{G},L_{2}(Q),\epsilon\left|\left|G\right|\right|_{L_{2}(Q)}\right)
is the
\epsilon\left|\left|G\right|\right|_{L_{2}(Q)}
-covering number of the (semi-)metric space
\left(\mathcal{G},\left|\left|\cdot\right|\right|_{L_{2}(Q)}\right)
,
Q
is any probability measure on
\mathbb{R}^{d+1}
,
G
is an envelope function of
\mathcal{G}
, and
\left|\left|G\right|\right|_{L_{2}(Q)}
is defined as
\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d+1}}\left[G(x)\right]^{2}\,dQ(x)\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}
.

Lemma C.2

(VC-type result related to the influence function
\varphi_{t}
)
.

Let
q\geq 2
in the local polynomial regression for estimating
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu(t,\bm{s})
and
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{T}
be a compact set so that
p_{T}(t)
is uniformly bounded away from 0 within
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
. Suppose that Assumptions
A3
,
A4
, and
A6
hold. Then, the class of scaled influence functions

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}=\left\{(v,x,\bm{z})\mapsto\sqrt{h^{3}}\cdot\varphi_{t}(v,x,\bm{z}):t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\right\}

has its covering number
N\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(Q),\epsilon\right)
and bracketing number
N_{[]}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(Q),2\epsilon\right)
as:

\sup_{Q}N\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(Q),\epsilon\right)\leq\sup_{Q}N_{[]}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(Q),2\epsilon\right)\leq\frac{C_{4}}{\epsilon}

for some constant
C_{4}>0
, where the supremum is taken over all probability measures
Q
for which the class
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
is not identically 0. In other words,
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
is a bounded VC-type class of functions with an envelope function
(v,x,\bm{z})\mapsto F_{1}(v,x,\bm{z})=C_{5}\cdot|v|
for some constant
C_{5}>0
. Analogously,

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}=\left\{(v,x,\bm{z})\mapsto\sqrt{h^{3}}\cdot\bar{\varphi}_{t}(v,x,\bm{z}):t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\right\}

is also a bounded VC-type class of functions with an envelope function
(v,x,\bm{z})\mapsto F_{1}(v,x,\bm{z})=C_{6}\cdot|v|
for some constant
C_{6}>0
. Here, the constants
C_{4},C_{5},C_{6}>0
are independent of
n,h,b,\hslash
.

Proof of Lemma
C.2
.

We assume without loss of generality that
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
is connected. Otherwise, we can focus our analysis on a connected component of
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
and take the union afterwards.

For any
f_{t_{1}},f_{t_{2}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
, we know from (
17
) that

\displaystyle\left|f_{t_{1}}(v,x,\bm{z})-f_{t_{2}}(v,x,\bm{z})\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sqrt{h^{3}}\left[\varphi_{t_{1}}(v,x,\bm{z})-\varphi_{t_{2}}(v,x,\bm{z})\right]\right|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left\{\int_{T_{1}}^{t_{1}}\frac{h\cdot C_{K_{T}}\left[v-\mu(x,\bm{z})\right]}{p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{x-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{x-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)d\widetilde{t}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left\{\int_{T_{1}}^{t_{2}}\frac{h\cdot C_{K_{T}}\left[v-\mu(x,\bm{z})\right]}{p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{x-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{x-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)d\widetilde{t}\right\}\Bigg|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{1}}\frac{h\cdot C_{K_{T}}\left[v-\mu(x,\bm{z})\right]}{p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{x-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{x-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)d\widetilde{t}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\left|t_{1}-t_{2}\right||v-\mu(x,z)|,

where the last inequality uses the boundedness of
\frac{1}{p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}
for any
\widetilde{t}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
and
\left(\frac{x-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{x-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)
for any
\widetilde{t}\in\mathcal{T}
under Assumption
A6
. Here,
C_{3}>0
is some constant that is independent of
n,h,b,\hslash
. Furthermore, we can also compute the
L_{2}(Q)
-norm of
(v,x,\bm{z})\mapsto C_{3}|v-\mu(x,z)|
for any probability measure as:

\displaystyle C_{3}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{Q}\left[|Y-\mu(T,\bm{Z})|^{2}\right]\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C_{3}\sigma<\infty

under Assumption
A3
. Since the diameter of
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
is finite, we conclude from Example 19.7 in
van der Vaart (1998)
that

\sup_{Q}N_{[]}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(Q),\epsilon\right)\leq\frac{C_{3}^{\prime}}{\epsilon}

for some constant
C_{3}^{\prime}>0
. Additionally, since any
2\epsilon
bracket
[f_{t_{1}},f_{t_{2}}]
of
\mathcal{F}
is contained in a ball of radius
\epsilon
centered at
\frac{f_{t_{1}}+f_{t_{2}}}{2}
, we know that

\sup_{Q}N\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(Q),\epsilon\right)\leq\sup_{Q}N_{[]}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(Q),2\epsilon\right)\leq\frac{C_{4}}{\epsilon}

for some constant
C_{4}>0
. Thus,
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
is a bounded VC-type class of functions. Finally, the above calculations tell us that for any
f_{t}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
,

\displaystyle\left|f_{t}(v,x,\bm{z})\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left\{\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\frac{h\cdot C_{K_{T}}\left[v-\mu(x,\bm{z})\right]}{p_{T}(\widetilde{t})}\left(\frac{x-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{x-\widetilde{t}}{h}\right)d\widetilde{t}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{T_{1}}\left[|t-T_{1}|\cdot|v-\mu(x,\bm{z})|\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{5}\cdot|v-\mu(x,\bm{z})|,

where
C_{5}>0
is some absolute constant. Thus, an envelope function of
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
can be given by
(v,x,\bm{z})\mapsto F_{1}(v,x,\bm{z})=C_{5}\cdot|v-\mu(x,\bm{z})|
. The result thus follows for
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
.

As for
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}
, we note that

\displaystyle g_{t}(v,x,\bm{z})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{h^{3}}\cdot\bar{\varphi}_{t}(v,x,\bm{z})

\displaystyle=\frac{h\cdot C_{K_{T}}[v-\mu(x,\bm{z})]}{p_{T}(t)}\left(\frac{x-t}{h}\right)K_{T}\left(\frac{x-t}{h}\right)

for each
g_{t}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}
. Furthermore, by Assumption
A6
with some fixed
\bm{z},\bm{s}\in\mathcal{S}
, we know that
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}
is a bounded VC-type class of functions. Moreover,
\left|g_{t}(v,x,\bm{z})\right|\leq C_{6}|v-\mu(x,\bm{z})|
for some constant
C_{6}>0
, which serves as an envelop function of
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}
with a finite
L_{2}(Q)
-norm for any probability measure
Q
. The result thus follows for
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}
.
∎

Lemma C.3

(Corollary 2.2 in
Chernozhukov et al. 2014
)
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a collection of functions that is pointwise measurable and of VC-type (see Assumption
A6
) with an envelop function
\widetilde{G}
and constants
A\geq e
and
\upsilon\geq 1
. Suppose also that for some
\widetilde{A}\geq\widetilde{\sigma}>0
and
q^{\prime}\in[4,\infty]
, we have
\sup_{\widetilde{g}\in\mathcal{G}}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{g}(\bm{U})^{k}\right]\leq\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}\cdot\widetilde{A}^{k-2}
for
k=2,3,4
and
\left|\left|G\right|\right|_{P,q^{\prime}}\leq\widetilde{A}
. Let
\mathbb{B}
be a centered Gaussian process defined on
\mathcal{G}
with covariance function

\mathrm{Cov}\left(\mathbb{B}(\widetilde{g}_{1}),\mathbb{B}(\widetilde{g}_{2})\right)=\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{g}_{1}(\bm{U})\cdot\widetilde{g}_{2}(\bm{U})\right],

where
\widetilde{g}_{1},\widetilde{g}_{2}\in\mathcal{G}
and
\bm{U}=(Y,T,\bm{S})
. Then, for every
\widetilde{\gamma}\in(0,1)
and sufficiently large, there exists a random variable
\widetilde{B}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\sup_{\widetilde{g}\in\mathcal{G}}\mathbb{B}(\widetilde{g})
such that

\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\left|\sup_{\widetilde{g}\in\mathcal{G}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}(\widetilde{g})\right|-\widetilde{B}\right|>\frac{C_{1}\cdot\widetilde{A}^{\frac{1}{3}}\widetilde{\sigma}^{\frac{2}{3}}\log^{\frac{2}{3}}n}{\widetilde{\gamma}^{\frac{1}{3}}n^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right)\leq C_{2}\cdot\widetilde{\gamma},

where
C_{1},C_{2}>0
are two constants that only depend on
q^{\prime}
. Here,
\widetilde{B}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}

\widetilde{B}_{2}
for two random variables
\widetilde{B}_{1},\widetilde{B}_{2}
means that they have the same distribution.

Lemma C.4

(Lemma 2.3 in
Chernozhukov et al. 2014
)
.

Under the same setup for
\mathcal{G}
as in Lemma
C.3
, we assume that there exist constants
\underline{\sigma},\bar{\sigma}>0
such that
\underline{\sigma}^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{g}^{2}(\bm{U})\right]\leq\bar{\sigma}^{2}
for all
\widetilde{g}\in\mathcal{G}
. Moreover, suppose that there exist constants
r_{1},r_{2}>0
such that

\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\left|\sup_{\widetilde{g}\in\mathcal{G}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|-\sup_{\widetilde{g}\in\mathcal{G}}|\mathbb{B}(f)|\right|>r_{1}\right)\leq r_{2}.

Then,

\displaystyle\sup_{u\geq 0}\left|\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sup_{\widetilde{g}\in\mathcal{G}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|\leq u\right)-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sup_{\widetilde{g}\in\mathcal{G}}|\mathbb{B}(f)|\geq u\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\sigma}r_{1}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\widetilde{g}\in\mathcal{G}}|\mathbb{B}(f)|\right]+\sqrt{\max\left\{1,\,\log\left(\frac{\underline{\sigma}}{r_{1}}\right)\right\}}\right\}+r_{2},

where
C_{\sigma}>0
is a constant depending only on
\underline{\sigma}
and
\bar{\sigma}
.

Lemma C.5
.

Let
d\geq 1
. If
b\lesssim h\asymp n^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}
and
\hslash\asymp n^{-\frac{1}{\varpi}}
for some
\gamma\geq\varpi>0
such that
\frac{nh^{5}}{\log n}\to c_{1}
and
\frac{n\hslash^{5}}{\log n}\to c_{2}
for some finite number
c_{1},c_{2}\geq 0
and
\frac{\hslash}{h^{3}\log n},\hslash n^{\frac{1}{3}}\log n,\frac{\sqrt{n\hslash}}{\log n},\frac{n\max\{h,\hslash\}b^{d}}{\log n}\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
, then

\max\left\{\sqrt{nh^{3}\max\{h,\hslash\}^{4}},\sqrt{\frac{h^{3}\log n}{\hslash}},\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n\hslash}}\right\}\lesssim\frac{\log^{\frac{2}{3}}n}{\left(nh^{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}}

(43)

when
n
is sufficiently large.

Proof of Lemma
C.5
.

We consider controlling the four quantities on the left hand side of (
43
) by the right hand side separately.

\bullet

Quantity I:
Note that
\sqrt{nh^{7}}\lesssim\frac{\log^{\frac{2}{3}}n}{\left(nh^{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}}
is equivalent to
nh^{6}\lesssim\log n
. Under our conditions, we know that the slowest rate of convergence for
h
is
O\left(n^{-\frac{1}{5}}\right)
up to some logarithmic factors of
n
. Thus,
nh^{6}=O\left(n^{-\frac{1}{5}}\right)
will be dominated by
O(\log n)
. Similarly,
\sqrt{nh^{3}\hslash^{4}}\lesssim\frac{\log^{\frac{2}{3}}n}{\left(nh^{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}}
is equivalent to
nh^{3}\hslash^{3}\lesssim\log n
. Under our conditions, we know that the slowest rate of convergence for
h
or
\hslash
is
O\left(n^{-\frac{1}{5}}\right)
up to some logarithmic factors of
n
. Thus,
nh^{3}\hslash^{3}=O\left(n^{-\frac{1}{5}}\right)
will be dominated by
O(\log n)
.

\bullet

Quantity II:
Note that
\sqrt{\frac{h^{3}\log n}{\hslash}}\lesssim\frac{\log^{\frac{2}{3}}n}{\left(nh^{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}}
is equivalent to
\frac{nh^{12}}{\hslash^{3}}\lesssim\log n
. Again, under our conditions, the slowest rates of convergence for
h
is
O\left(n^{-\frac{1}{5}}\right)
, and the fastest rate of convergence for
\hslash
is smaller than the order
O\left(n^{-\frac{1}{3}}\right)
up to some logarithmic factors of
n
. Under these rates,
\frac{nh^{12}}{\hslash^{3}}=O\left(n^{-\frac{2}{5}}\right)
will be dominated by
O\left(\log n\right)
.

\bullet

Quantity III:
Note that
\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n\hslash}}\lesssim\frac{\log^{\frac{2}{3}}n}{\left(nh^{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}}
is equivalent to
\frac{h^{3}}{n^{2}\hslash^{3}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\log^{2}n}
. Again, under our conditions, the slowest rate of convergence for
h
is
O\left(n^{-\frac{1}{5}}\right)
up to some logarithmic factors of
n
. Furthermore, given that
\hslash n^{\frac{1}{3}}\log n\to\infty
, the fastest rate of convergence for
\hslash
should be smaller than the order
O\left(n^{-\frac{1}{3}}\right)
. Thus, under these rates,
\frac{h^{3}}{n^{2}\hslash^{3}}
is dominated by
O\left(n^{-\frac{8}{5}}\right)
and thus by
O\left(\frac{1}{\log^{2}n}\right)
.

In summary, the result follows from combining the above four cases.
∎

Theorem 6

(Gaussian approximation)
.

Let
q\geq 2
in the local polynomial regression for estimating
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu(t,\bm{s})
and
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{T}
be a compact set so that
p_{T}(t)
is uniformly bounded away from 0 within
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
. Suppose that Assumptions
A1
,
A2
,
A3
,
A4
,
A5
, and
A6
hold. If
b\lesssim h\asymp n^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}
and
\hslash\asymp n^{-\frac{1}{\varpi}}
for some
\gamma\geq\varpi>0
such that
\frac{nh^{5}}{\log n}\to c_{1}
and
\frac{n\hslash^{5}}{\log n}\to c_{2}
for some finite number
c_{1},c_{2}\geq 0
and
\frac{\hslash}{h^{3}\log n},\hslash n^{\frac{1}{3}}\log n,\frac{\sqrt{n\hslash}}{\log n},\frac{n\max\{h,\hslash\}b^{d}}{\log n}\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
, then there exist Gaussian processes
\mathbb{B},\bar{\mathbb{B}}
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{u\geq 0}\left|\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}}\cdot\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)\right|\leq u\right)-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}(f)|\leq u\right)\right|

\displaystyle\asymp\sup_{u\geq 0}\left|\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}}\cdot\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)-\theta_{C}(t)\right|\leq u\right)-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}_{\theta}}|\bar{\mathbb{B}}(g)|\leq u\right)\right|

\displaystyle=O\left(\left(\frac{\log^{5}n}{nh^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{8}}+\left(\frac{\log^{2}n}{nb^{d}\hslash}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}}\right),

where
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}_{\theta}
are defined in (
18
).

Proof of
Theorem 6
.

We only prove the Gaussian approximation for
\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)
, since the result for its derivative estimator
\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)
follows from an identical argument. At a high level, given the asymptotic linearity of
\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)
in Lemma
5
, we will use Lemma
C.3
to establish the coupling between
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|
and
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}(f)|
for the Gaussian process
\mathbb{B}
defined in the theorem statement and then utilize Lemma
C.4
to translate the coupling to a bound on the Kolmogorov distance between
\sqrt{nh^{3}}\cdot\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)\right|
and
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}(f)|
.

By Lemma
C.2
, we know that the class of scaled influence functions

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}=\left\{(v,x,\bm{z})\mapsto\sqrt{h^{3}}\cdot\varphi_{t}(v,x,\bm{z}):t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\right\}=\left\{\sqrt{h^{3}}\cdot f:f\in\mathcal{F}\right\}

is a VC-type class with an envelope function
(v,x,\bm{z})\mapsto F_{1}(v,x,\bm{z})=C_{5}\cdot|v-\mu(x,\bm{z})|
for some constant
C_{5}>0
that is independent of
n,h,b,\hslash
. In addition, recalling the definition of
\varphi_{t}
in (
17
) together with our Assumption
A6
on moments of the kernel functions
K_{T}
and Lemma
C.1
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbb{E}\left[f(\bm{U})^{2}\right]\leq C_{6}^{2}\cdot h^{3}:=\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}<\infty\quad\text{ and }\quad\left[\mathbb{E}\left(C_{5}|Y-\mu(T,\bm{S})|\right)^{4}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq C_{5}^{\prime}\left(\mathbb{E}|\epsilon|^{4}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}:=\widetilde{A}<\infty,

where
C_{5}^{\prime},C_{6}>0
are some constants that are independent of
n,h,b,\hslash
. By Lemma
C.3
, we know that for any
\widetilde{\gamma}\in(0,1)
,

\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\left|\sup_{f\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|-\sup_{f\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}|\mathbb{B}(f)|\right|>\frac{C_{1}\cdot\widetilde{A}^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(h^{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\log^{\frac{2}{3}}n}{\widetilde{\gamma}^{\frac{1}{3}}n^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right)\leq\frac{C_{2}\cdot\widetilde{\gamma}}{2}.

Dividing
\sqrt{h^{3}}
on both sides of the inequality inside
\mathrm{P}
gives us that

\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\left|\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}(\varphi_{t})|-\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}(f)|\right|>\frac{C_{1}\cdot\widetilde{A}^{\frac{1}{3}}\log^{\frac{2}{3}}n}{\widetilde{\gamma}^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(nh^{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right)\leq\frac{C_{2}\cdot\widetilde{\gamma}}{2}.

On the other hand, we know from Lemma
5
that for any
\widetilde{\gamma}\in(0,1)
,

\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\frac{\left|\sqrt{nh^{3}}\cdot\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)\right|-\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}\varphi_{t}|\right|}{\max\left\{\sqrt{nh^{3}\max\{h,\hslash\}^{4}},\sqrt{\frac{h^{3}\log n}{\hslash}},\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n\hslash}},\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{nb^{d}\hslash}}\right\}}>\frac{C_{3}}{\widetilde{\gamma}^{\frac{1}{3}}}\right)\leq\frac{C_{2}\cdot\widetilde{\gamma}}{2},

where
C_{3}>0
is some large constant. Combining the above two inequalities yields that

\displaystyle\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\Bigg(\left|\sqrt{nh^{3}}\cdot\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)\right|-\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}(f)|\right|>

\displaystyle\quad\frac{C_{3}}{\widetilde{\gamma}^{\frac{1}{3}}}\cdot\max\left\{\sqrt{nh^{3}\max\{h,\hslash\}^{4}},\sqrt{\frac{h^{3}\log n}{\hslash}},\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n\hslash}},\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{nb^{d}\hslash}}\right\}+\frac{C_{1}^{\prime}\cdot\log^{\frac{2}{3}}n}{\widetilde{\gamma}^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(nh^{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}}\Bigg)\leq C_{2}\cdot\widetilde{\gamma},

where
C_{1}^{\prime}=C_{1}\cdot\widetilde{A}^{\frac{1}{3}}>0
is again a constant. Now, if
b\lesssim h\asymp n^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}
and
\hslash\asymp n^{-\frac{1}{\varpi}}
for some
\gamma\geq\varpi>0
such that
\frac{nh^{5}}{\log n}\to c_{1}
and
\frac{n\hslash^{5}}{\log n}\to c_{2}
for some finite number
c_{1},c_{2}\geq 0
and
\frac{\hslash}{h^{3}\log n},\hslash n^{\frac{1}{3}}\log n,\frac{\sqrt{n\hslash}}{\log n},\frac{n\max\{h,\hslash\}b^{d}}{\log n}\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
, then we know from Lemma
C.5
that

\max\left\{\sqrt{nh^{3}\max\{h,\hslash\}^{4}},\sqrt{\frac{h^{3}\log n}{\hslash}},\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n\hslash}}\right\}\leq\frac{C_{1}^{\prime}\cdot\log^{\frac{2}{3}}n}{\left(nh^{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}}

when
n
is sufficiently large. Hence, we conclude that when
n
is sufficiently large,

\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\left|\sqrt{nh^{3}}\cdot\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)\right|-\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}(f)|\right|>\frac{C_{4}}{\widetilde{\gamma}^{\frac{1}{3}}}\left[\frac{\log^{\frac{2}{3}}}{\left(nh^{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{nb^{d}\hslash}}\right]\right)\leq C_{2}\cdot\widetilde{\gamma},

for some large constants
C_{2},C_{4}>0
. To upper bound the Kolmogorov distance between
\sqrt{nh^{3}}\cdot\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)\right|
and
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}(f)|
, we leverage Lemmas
C.4
and
C.1
to obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{u\geq 0}\left|\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}}\cdot\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)\right|\leq u\right)-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}(f)|\leq u\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{5}}{\widetilde{\gamma}^{\frac{1}{3}}}\left[\frac{\log^{\frac{5}{6}}}{\left(nh^{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}}+\frac{\log n}{(nb^{d}\hslash)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right]+C_{2}\cdot\widetilde{\gamma},

where
C_{5}>0
is some constant that depends only on
\bar{\sigma}\geq\underline{\sigma}>0
in Lemma
C.1
. Here, we also utilize the fact that
\log\left(\frac{1}{r_{1}}\right)=\log n
when
r_{1}=\frac{C_{4}}{\widetilde{\gamma}^{\frac{1}{3}}}\left[\frac{\log^{\frac{2}{3}}}{\left(nh^{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{nb^{d}\hslash}}\right]
and use the Dudley’s entropy inequality for Gaussian processes (Corollary 2.2.8 in
van der Vaart and Wellner 1996
) to argue that
\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}(f)|\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}|\mathbb{B}(\varphi_{t})|\right]=O\left(\sqrt{\log n}\right)
. We take
\widetilde{\gamma}=O\left(\left(\frac{\log^{5}n}{nh^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{8}}+\left(\frac{\log^{2}n}{nb^{d}\hslash}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}}\right)
to optimize the right hand side of the above inequality and deduce that

\displaystyle\sup_{u\geq 0}\left|\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}}\cdot\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)\right|\leq u\right)-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}(f)|\leq u\right)\right|=O\left(\left(\frac{\log^{5}n}{nh^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{8}}+\left(\frac{\log^{2}n}{nb^{d}\hslash}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}}\right).

The result follows.
∎

C.6
Proof of
Theorem 7

Theorem 7

(Bootstrap consistency)
.

Let
q\geq 2
in the local polynomial regression for estimating
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu(t,\bm{s})
,
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{T}
be a compact set so that
p_{T}(t)
is uniformly bounded away from 0 within
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
, and
\mathbb{U}_{n}=\left\{(Y_{i},T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})\right\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the observed data. Suppose that Assumptions
A1
,
A2
,
A3
,
A4
,
A5
, and
A6
hold. If
b\lesssim h\asymp n^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}
and
\hslash\asymp n^{-\frac{1}{\varpi}}
for some
\gamma,\varpi>0
such that
\frac{nh^{5}}{\log n}\to c_{1}
and
\frac{n\hslash^{5}}{\log n}\to c_{2}
for some finite number
c_{1},c_{2}\geq 0
and
\frac{\hslash}{h^{3}\log n},\hslash n^{\frac{1}{3}}\log n,\frac{\sqrt{n\hslash}}{\log n},\frac{n\max\{h,\hslash\}b^{d}}{\log n}\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
, then

\displaystyle\sup_{u\geq 0}\left|\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}}\cdot\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{m}_{\theta}^{*}(t)-\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)\right|\leq u\Big|\mathbb{U}_{n}\right)-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}(f)|\leq u\right)\right|

\displaystyle\asymp\sup_{u\geq 0}\left|\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}}\cdot\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{\theta}_{C}^{*}(t)-\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)\right|\leq u\Big|\mathbb{U}_{n}\right)-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}_{\theta}}|\bar{\mathbb{B}}(g)|\leq u\right)\right|

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(\left(\frac{\log^{5}n}{nh^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{8}}+\left(\frac{\log^{2}n}{nb^{d}\hslash}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}}\right),

where
\widehat{m}_{\theta}^{*}(t)
and
\widehat{\theta}_{C}^{*}(t)
are the integral estimator (
10
) and localized derivative estimator (
14
) based on a bootstrap sample
\mathbb{U}_{n}^{*}=\left\{\left(Y_{i}^{*},T_{i}^{*},\bm{S}_{i}^{*}\right)\right\}_{i=1}^{n}
respectively, and
\mathbb{B},\bar{\mathbb{B}}
are the same Gaussian processes as in
Theorem 6
.

Proof of
Theorem 7
.

We only prove the bootstrap consistency for
\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)
, since the result for its derivative estimator
\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)
follows from an identical argument. Our proof here is similar to the proof of Theorem 7 in
Chen et al. (2015)
and the proof of Theorem 4 in
Chen et al. (2017)
. The key difference is that the functional space remains unchanged as
\mathcal{F}
in our scenario here for both the original Gaussian approximation (
Theorem 6
) and the bootstrapped version, because the index set
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{F}
is fixed.

Let
\mathbb{U}_{n}=\left\{(Y_{i},T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})\right\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the observed data and
\mathbb{U}_{n}^{*}=\left\{(Y_{i}^{*},T_{i}^{*},\bm{S}_{i}^{*})\right\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the bootstrap sample. We also denote
\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}(\mathbb{U}_{n})=\sqrt{n}\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\right)
, where
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}
is the empirical measure defined by the bootstrap sample
\mathbb{U}_{n}^{*}
. Assume that
\mathbb{U}_{n}\subset\mathcal{Y}\times\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{S}
is fixed for a moment. Then, we can apply our arguments in Lemma
5
by replacing the probability measure
\mathrm{P}
by
\mathbb{P}_{n}
and obtain that

\displaystyle\left|\sqrt{nh^{3}}\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{m}_{\theta}^{*}(t)-\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)\right|-\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}(\mathbb{U}_{n})\varphi_{t}|\right|

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}\max\{h,\hslash\}^{4}}+\sqrt{\frac{h^{3}\log n}{\hslash}}+\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n\hslash}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{nb^{d}\hslash}}\right).

Following the same argument in
Theorem 6
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{u\geq 0}\left|\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}}\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{m}_{\theta}^{*}(t)-\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)\right|\leq u\Big|\mathbb{U}_{n}\right)-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}_{n}(f)|\leq u\Big|\mathbb{U}_{n}\right)\right|\\
&=O_{P}\left(\left(\frac{\log^{5}n}{nh^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{8}}+\left(\frac{\log^{2}n}{nb^{d}\hslash}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}}\right),\end{split}

(44)

where
\mathbb{B}_{n}
is a Gaussian process on
\mathcal{F}
such that for any
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathcal{F}
, it has

\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{B}_{n}(f_{1})|\mathbb{U}_{n}\right]=0\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathrm{Cov}\left[\mathbb{B}_{n}(f_{1}),\mathbb{B}_{n}(f_{2})\big|\mathbb{U}_{n}\right]=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{1}(Y_{i},T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})\cdot f_{2}(Y_{i},T_{i},\bm{S}_{i}).

Notice that the difference between
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}_{n}(f)|
and
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}(f)|
is small, because these two Gaussian processes differ in their covariance and

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}\left[\mathbb{B}_{n}(f_{1}),\mathbb{B}_{n}(f_{2})\big|\mathbb{U}_{n}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{1}(Y_{i},T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})\cdot f_{2}(Y_{i},T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{Cov}(\mathbb{B}(f_{1}),\mathbb{B}(f_{2}))=\mathbb{E}\left[f_{1}(Y,T,\bm{S})\cdot f_{2}(Y,T,\bm{S})\right]

as
n\to\infty
. More precisely, by Corollary 9 in
Giessing (2023)
, we know that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{u\geq 0}\left|\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}_{n}(f)|\leq u\Big|\mathbb{U}_{n}\right)-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}(f)|\leq u\right)\right|\\
&\leq C_{6}\left[\frac{\sup_{f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\mathrm{Cov}\left[\mathbb{B}_{n}(f_{1}),\mathbb{B}_{n}(f_{2})\big|\mathbb{U}_{n}\right]-\mathrm{Cov}\left[\mathbb{B}(f_{1}),\mathbb{B}(f_{2})\right]\right|}{\max\left\{\mathrm{Var}\left(\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}(f)|\right),\mathrm{Var}\left(\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}_{n}(f)|\right)\right\}}\right]^{\frac{1}{3}}\\
&\leq C_{6}^{\prime}\left[\sup_{f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{1}(Y_{i},T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})\cdot f_{2}(Y_{i},T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})-\mathbb{E}\left[f_{1}(Y,T,\bm{S})\cdot f_{2}(Y,T,\bm{S})\right]\right|\right]^{\frac{1}{3}}\\
&=C_{6}^{\prime}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{2}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f(Y_{i},T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})-\mathbb{E}\left[f(Y,T,\bm{S})\right]\right|\right]^{\frac{1}{3}}\end{split}

(45)

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
C.1
and
C_{6},C_{6}^{\prime}>0
are two absolute constants. Here,
\mathcal{F}^{2}\equiv\left\{f_{1}\cdot f_{2}:f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathcal{F}\right\}
. Now, by symmetrization (Lemma 2.3.1 in
van der Vaart and Wellner 1996
) and maximal inequality (Corollary 2.2.8 in
van der Vaart and Wellner 1996
), we also know that

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{2}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f(Y_{i},T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})-\mathbb{E}\left[f(Y,T,\bm{S})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{2}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\chi_{i}f(Y_{i},T_{i},\bm{S}_{i})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\sup_{Q}\sqrt{\log N\left(\mathcal{F}^{2},L_{2}(Q),\epsilon\right)}\,d\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{7}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\sup_{Q}\sqrt{\log N\left(\mathcal{F},L_{2}(Q),\epsilon\right)}\,d\epsilon

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{7}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{0}^{\sqrt{h^{3}}\cdot M_{\mathcal{F}}}\sup_{Q}\sqrt{\log N\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(Q),\sqrt{h^{3}}\cdot\epsilon\right)}\,d\epsilon

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{7}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{nh^{3}}}\int_{0}^{M_{\mathcal{F}}}\sup_{Q}\sqrt{\log N\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(Q),u\right)}\,du

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{8}}{\sqrt{nh^{3}}},

where
\chi_{1},...,\chi_{n}
are Rademacher random variables,
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}=\sqrt{h^{3}}\cdot\mathcal{F}=\left\{\sqrt{h^{3}}f:f\in\mathcal{F}\right\}
by Lemma
C.2
, and
C_{7},C_{7}^{\prime},C_{8}>0
are absolute constants. In addition,
M_{\mathcal{F}}=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[f(Y_{1},T_{1},\bm{S}_{1})^{2}\right]<\infty
by Lemma
C.1
and the last inequality follows from the VC-type property of
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
by Lemma
C.2
. Combining the above result with (
45
), we obtain that

\sup_{u\geq 0}\left|\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}_{n}(f)|\leq u\Big|\mathbb{U}_{n}\right)-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{B}(f)|\leq u\right)\right|=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\left(nh^{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right).

Together with (
44
), the result thus follows.
∎

C.7
Proof of Corollary
8

Corollary 8

(Uniform confidence band)
.

Under the setup of
Theorem 7
, we have that

\displaystyle\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\theta(t)\in\left[\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)-\bar{\xi}_{1-\alpha}^{*},\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)+\bar{\xi}_{1-\alpha}^{*}\right]\text{ for all }t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\right)=1-\alpha+O\left(\left(\frac{\log^{5}n}{nh^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{8}}+\left(\frac{\log^{2}n}{nb^{d}\hslash}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}}\right),

\displaystyle\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(m(t)\in\left[\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-\xi_{1-\alpha}^{*},\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)+\xi_{1-\alpha}^{*}\right]\text{ for all }t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\right)=1-\alpha+O\left(\left(\frac{\log^{5}n}{nh^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{8}}+\left(\frac{\log^{2}n}{nb^{d}\hslash}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}}\right).

Proof of Corollary
8
.

By
Theorem 6
and
Theorem 7
, we have the Berry-Esseen bounds related to bootstrap estimates for the distributions of
\sqrt{nh^{3}}\cdot\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)\right|
and
\sqrt{nh^{3}}\cdot\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)-\theta_{C}(t)\right|
as:

\displaystyle\sup_{u\geq 0}\left|\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}}\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{m}_{\theta}^{*}(t)-\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)\right|\leq u\Big|\mathbb{U}_{n}\right)-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}}\cdot\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{m}_{\theta}(t)-m(t)\right|\leq u\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad=O_{P}\left(\left(\frac{\log^{5}n}{nh^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{8}}+\left(\frac{\log^{2}n}{nb^{d}\hslash}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}}\right),

and

\displaystyle\sup_{u\geq 0}\left|\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}}\cdot\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{\theta}_{C}^{*}(t)-\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)\right|\leq u\right)-\mbox{$\mathrm{P}$}\left(\sqrt{nh^{3}}\cdot\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left|\widehat{\theta}_{C}(t)-\theta_{C}(t)\right|\leq u\right)\right|

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(\left(\frac{\log^{5}n}{nh^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{8}}+\left(\frac{\log^{2}n}{nb^{d}\hslash}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}}\right).

The results thus follow.
∎
